  # PART 4 â€” THE SESSION SYSTEM (DEEP DIVE)

  ## 4.1 What a session is

  A session is the core user experience: a structured, time-bound practice (typically
  5â€“60 minutes) with four phases and a fixed time formula (`getSessionFormula()`):

  1. **Teaching (30%)** â€” the guide/master introduces today's theme (2â€“5 min, pointed,
    never a lecture).
  2. **Practice (50%)** â€” an interactive practice selected by the Practice Engine:
    breath, body, self-inquiry, witness, emotional, shadow, relationship, decision,
    silence, or three-brains work â€” always *doing*, never passive video.
  3. **Reflection (20%)** â€” chat ("What arose for you?"), journal prompt, and a
    self-rating ("How present were you? 0â€“100%").
  4. **Integration** â€” an action item for the day, a micro-practice for later, and a
    preview of the next session; karma/XP awarded.

  Sessions live in the `sessions` table (unified: `type` = guide/generative/â€¦,
  `time_slot` = morning/midday/evening/night, `status` = active/completed, `summary`
  JSON), with chat transcripts in `session_messages`, and the v3 pipeline's durable record
  in `session_spine` (Â§4.8).

  ## 4.2 The four daily gates

  The day is organized into four time gates, each with its own character
  (`dailyGates.ts`, `dailyCycleMachine`, the `--slot-*` CSS gradients):

  | Gate | Hours | Character | Practices | AI tone |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Morning | 5amâ€“12pm | intention, new beginnings | breath, activation, energizing | fresh, encouraging, clear |
  | Midday | 12pmâ€“5pm | awareness in action | micro-practices (60s), body awareness | light, supportive, non-intrusive |
  | Evening | 5pmâ€“9pm | integration, reflection, release | longer sessions, emotional work, journaling | deeper, more spacious |
  | Night Sanctuary | 9pmâ€“5am | rest, surrender, dream | body scan, yoga nidra, dream journal | minimal, soft, almost silent |

  `gateForHour()` (night < 5, morning < 12, midday < 17, evening < 21, else night) is the
  canonical mapping used by `getUserContext()`, the composer, and the session flow.

  ## 4.3 Session lifecycle end-to-end

  ```text
  Home orb (press-and-hold 1.5s ritual)
    â””â”€ startGenerativeSession({ intent, slot, themeId, timeBudget, energy })   [session-shell/api.ts]
        â”œâ”€ ensureCreditsForSession()          # user_credits.balance_paise â‰¥ 50, else 402 + upsell
        â”œâ”€ [flag off] generate-session-agenda  # legacy composer (production default)
        â”œâ”€ [flag on]  compose-session-v3       # Sankalpa Engine â†’ plan + spineId (Â§3.5.3)
        â””â”€ SessionShellProvider mounts the plan; cursor = -1 (agenda card)
    â””â”€ beginFromAgenda() â†’ cursor 0..N-1 through steps
        â”œâ”€ each step: kind-specific UI (wisdom-read / breathwork / journal / micro-act /
        â”‚   reflection / guide-chat / practice / temple-chat / witness)
        â”œâ”€ goToNext(payload) records duration+payload into completedSteps
        â”œâ”€ skipCurrent() records into skippedSteps
        â””â”€ progress persisted to localStorage 'session-shell:{sessionId}' every change
            â†’ an interrupted session resumes exactly where left off
    â””â”€ cursor = N â†’ completeGenerativeSession({ completedSteps, skippedSteps, totalSeconds })
        â”œâ”€ session-complete edge fn  â†’ writes unified `sessions` row + summary
        â”œâ”€ fire-and-forget award_karma_event RPC (base score from step count + reflective bonus)
        â””â”€ scoreSessionSpine({ spineId, finalUserSentence, stepCompletions, duration, mood })
              â†’ score-session-spine edge fn â†’ session_spine.quality_score
              â†’ read back by compose-session-v3's "adaptive history" next session
  ```

  Meanwhile, the chat-flavored session path (Guide tab) runs the parallel G2 stack
  (Â§4.6), and practice runs can be triggered from anywhere via the cinematic provider
  (Â§4.12). Signals emitted during all of this (mood pulses, reflections, quality
  ratings, bio/work/device inputs) land in `consciousness_signals` /
  `engine_signals` and close the loop into tomorrow's `getUserContext()`.

  ## 4.4 The four parallel session architectures

  This is the most important thing to understand about the session system: **it is four
  generations of architecture, all still live in the tree** (see also Â§12.1):

  | Gen | Runtime | Composition | Persistence | Status |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | **G1 Orb** | `sessionOrchestrator.ts` + localStorage (`orb-sessions-YYYY-M-D`) | deterministic `orchestrateSession()` (Â§4.10.2) | localStorage only | legacy/parallel surface |
  | **G2 Chat** | `sessionMachine` + `useGuideChat` + `useSessionFlow` | `promptDirector` + `_shared/adaptive-engine.ts` per turn; first message scripted by session flow (Â§4.5) | `sessions` + `session_messages` (+ legacy `chat_sessions`/`guide_messages` tables) | production (Guide tab) |
  | **G3 Generative** | `SessionShellProvider` cursor runtime | `generate-session-agenda` (legacy) or `compose-session-v3` (flag `sankalpaV3`) | `sessions.summary` + `session_spine` + `user_memory_vectors` | production (flagged v3) |
  | **G4 Cinematic** | `CinematicSessionProvider` + `PracticeRunControllerContext` + `sessionPlayerMachine` | scripted `CinematicScript`s resolved by practice level | practice run records | production (practice runs) |

  They share the same vocabulary (gate, archetype, step, spine, karma) and the same
  `getUserContext()` substrate â€” but they are distinct code paths, not one pipeline.

  ## 4.5 Session Flow Orchestrator: morning / evening / night scripts

  **Files:** `src/lib/sessionFlow.ts`, `src/hooks/useSessionFlow.ts`, `src/pages/Guide.tsx`
  (documented in `SESSION_FLOW.md`).

  Historical note (in the file's own header): this system *replaced* rigid fixed scripts
  (Morning 9-step / Evening 8-step / Night 5-phase) with a **context + state driven
  directive** â€” the client emits a high-level surface directive and the edge function's
  `_shared/adaptive-engine.ts` has "the final word" per turn.

  **Morning Session Flow (5amâ€“12pm)** â€” phases: (1) greeting (name, streak, journey day),
  (2) theme introduction ("Today's theme is {title}"), (3) why-this-theme (connected to
  band level, focus dimension, patterns), (4) how-this-helps (2â€“3 specific benefits tied
  to real life), (5) opening question ("How did you sleep? Any dreams?"), (6) practice
  suggestion after 2â€“3 exchanges, (7) integration ("What's one thing you're carrying
  forward?"). Special scenarios: **returning after 3+ days** â†’ no guilt, 5 minutes only,
  "the path celebrates return"; **streak 7+** â†’ challenge deeper, extend practice;
  otherwise the full structured flow.

  **Evening Session Flow (5pmâ€“9pm)** â€” phases: greeting + day review ("Good evening. The
  day is settling."), reflection on specifics (references the morning session, tasks,
  practice), celebrate wins ("even ONE moment of awareness is huge"), explore struggles
  without guilt, gratitude practice (3 things), lessons extraction ("What did today teach
  you?"), connect to tomorrow (preview next theme + one commitment), closing review
  (theme, clarity moment, lesson, gratitude).

  **Night Sanctuary Flow (9pmâ€“5am)** â€” hard rules: **no deep inquiry, no analysis,
  everything short and gentle, redirect to rest if the user starts analyzing.** Phases:
  wind-down greeting â†’ quick body check-in ("How's your body? Tired? Restless?") â†’
  state-based practice (restless â†’ 15 min Yoga Nidra; peaceful â†’ 5 min gratitude; heavy â†’
  10 min gentle breathwork) â†’ plant one seed (theme affirmation for sleep) â†’ closing
  ("Sleep well. The path holds you.").

  **Context data used** (`SessionFlowContext`): user (name, streak, journey day, band
  level/name, focus dimension, language), theme (title, description, rationale,
  learning focus, intentions, action items, affirmation, focus areas), morning-session
  results (completed, summary, insights), day activity (journal entries, practice done,
  tasks completed/missed, dominant mood, energy), missed days, yesterday's summary.

  **Wiring in Guide.tsx:** the hook builds system prompt + first message; the first
  message is injected as a scripted `STREAM_DELTA` when the session starts
  (`messageCount === 0`), so even before the user types anything, the guide *opens* the
  session in its own voice; all subsequent turns stream from `guide-chat` with
  `aiContext: sessionFlow.systemPrompt`.

  ## 4.6 Chat sessions: `sessionMachine` + streaming

  **Files:** `src/machines/sessionMachine.ts` (139 lines), `src/hooks/useGuideChat.ts`,
  `supabase/functions/guide-chat/index.ts`.

  `sessionMachine` models the live Guide chat end-to-end:

  - States: `idle â†’ starting â†’ active â†’ ending â†’ completed â†’ (RESET) idle`.
  - Context: `{ sessionId, timeSlot, themeId, themeName, messages: {role, content}[], error, isStreaming }`.
  - Events: `START_SESSION`, `SESSION_CREATED`, `ADD_USER_MESSAGE`, `START_STREAMING`,
    `STREAM_DELTA`, `STREAM_DONE`, `END_SESSION`/`SESSION_ENDED`, `ERROR`, `RESET`.
  - The canonical streaming reducer:

  ```ts
  STREAM_DELTA: {
    actions: assign({
      messages: ({ context, event }) => {
        const msgs = [...context.messages];
        const last = msgs[msgs.length - 1];
        if (last?.role === 'assistant') {
          msgs[msgs.length - 1] = { ...last, content: last.content + event.content };
        }
        return msgs;
      },
    }),
  },
  ```

  **Typewriter smoothing** (`useGuideChat.ts`): rather than pushing raw SSE chunks into
  React (bursty), incoming text appends to a `pending` buffer and a flush timer releases
  **2 characters every 18ms (~110 chars/sec)** regardless of chunk arrival â€” a
  ChatGPT-like progressive reveal even when the gateway flushes large chunks at once.

  **Guest-tag parsing:** a separate parser strips/holds `[GUEST:masterId]â€¦[/GUEST]`
  control sequences (Â§3.8.5), buffering partial bracket sequences in a `tagBuffer` so a
  tag split across chunks never leaks literal `[GUEST:` text on screen; fires
  `onSpeakerChange(speakerId|null)` when a guest starts/stops speaking. Only
  tag-stripped visible text feeds `onDelta` â†’ `STREAM_DELTA`. Karma is awarded as part of
  the same hook on completion.

  **Server side (`guide-chat`)**: canonical edge-function anatomy (Â§5.2) â€” auth â†’ atomic
  credit pre-check (50 paise) â†’ build the system prompt from `buildElderVoice()` +
  per-master dossier (`_shared/voice-lineage.ts`) + adaptive decision
  (`_shared/adaptive-engine.ts`) + long-term-memory block (Â§3.9.2) + human-presence
  fragment + kundli context â†’ stream the completion back as SSE â†’ on first message of a
  session it also internally calls **`session-adapter`** (function-to-function over
  HTTP, forwarding the auth header) so master/mode/practice adaptation is logged and
  auditable (Â§3.6.4) â†’ post-call billing via `computeChargedPaise()`.

  ## 4.7 The session-shell generative runtime

  **Files:** `src/lib/session-shell/{types.ts, api.ts, SessionShellContext.tsx}` â€” the
  actual UI-facing consumer of composed plans (both `generate-session-agenda` and
  `compose-session-v3`).

  - **`types.ts`** mirrors the edge payloads 1:1: `StepKind` (9-way union), `SessionStep`
    (kind, title, prompt, params, optional, est_minutes, whyForYou, signalRef),
    `SessionPlanMeta` (vLevelStart, dimensionBandsStart, composedBy, languageRefinedBy,
    archetype, archetypeReason, toneWords, whyThisSession), `SessionPlan`,
    `CompletedStepRecord`, `DimensionNudge`, `SessionCompleteResult` (ok, sessionId,
    completedStepCount, durationMs, loopsTouched, karmaEarned, shiftSummary, nextTeaser,
    vLevelDelta, dimensionNudges, qualityScore, primaryPractice).
  - **`api.ts`**:
    - `startGenerativeSession()` â€” credits gate â†’ feature flag `sankalpaV3Enabled()` â†’
      v3 (returns `sessionId: spineId ?? plan.id`) with **silent fallback** to the legacy
      agenda function on any v3 failure ("zero breakage").
    - `loadGenerativeSession(sessionId)` â€” rehydrates a plan from `sessions.summary`.
    - `completeGenerativeSession()` â€” `session-complete` + fire-and-forget karma RPC.
    - `scoreSessionSpine()` â€” `score-session-spine` (safe/fire-and-forget).
  - **`SessionShellContext.tsx`** â€” cursor semantics: `-1` = agenda card, `0..N-1` =
    step index, `N` = ready-to-complete. Persists to localStorage
    (`session-shell:{sessionId}`) on every state change; `readPersistedShell` /
    `clearPersistedShell` are exported so other surfaces (e.g. Home) can peek at
    in-progress state. Actions: `beginFromAgenda()`, `goToNext(payload?)` (records the
    current step's duration + payload into `completedSteps`), `skipCurrent()` (records
    into `skippedSteps`). `useSessionShell()` throws outside the provider.

  ## 4.8 Session completion, karma, and spine scoring

  - **`session-complete`** â€” writes the unified `sessions` row with a JSON summary
    (plan, steps completed/skipped, durations), returns the completion result payload,
    and computes `loopsTouched` (which active loops this session addressed â€” closing the
    loop-detector â†’ session loop).
  - **Karma** â€” `award_karma_event` RPC, base score from step count + a bonus for
    reflective steps; events accumulate in `karma_events` / `user_karma` /
    `xp_transactions` (the gamification layer behind `KarmaChip` and the constellation).
  - **`score-session-spine`** (Sankalpa T5) â€” scores the `session_spine` row for the
    session (inputs: the user's final sentence, per-step completions, duration, mood) and
    writes `quality_score`. This is the exact column compose-session-v3's **adaptive
    history** reads (Â§3.5.3 step 3) â€” archetype/master resonance statistics that bias
    (never force) future archetype selection. **The session system is a self-scoring
    feedback loop.**

  ## 4.9 Mid-session adaptation

  Two distinct adaptation moments:

  - **At entry:** `session-adapter` (Â§3.6.4) adapts the pre-assigned
    `{master, mode, practice}` once, at session start.
  - **Mid-session:** **`adapt-next-block`** runs between blocks. It pulls `engine_signals`
    from the last 15 minutes; if none, it fast-paths to `keep` with **zero AI cost**.
    Otherwise it hands the AI a strict tool call (`decide_adaptation`) with exactly three
    actions â€” `keep` (default), `replace` the next block, or `inject` a short 60â€“240s
    bridge step â€” constrained to a fixed practice vocabulary (`PRACTICE_ROUTE`:
    observation, breath, witness, body, self-inquiry, bhakti, jnana, journal) so the AI
    cannot hallucinate an unroutable practice. The system prompt biases toward `keep` and
    forbids injecting more than one extra step.

  ## 4.10 `src/lib/session/*` â€” the structured session library

  A 12-file typed library (with its own `IMPLEMENTATION.md`) that appears to be the
  newest consolidation layer for structured sessions:

  | File | Role |
  |---|---|
  | `types.ts` | canonical session types |
  | `orchestrator.ts` | session orchestration (successor of the G1 orb planner) |
  | `thread.ts` | session threading (continuity across days) |
  | `journey.ts` | journey-linked session progression |
  | `adapters.ts` | adapters between plan shapes and runtimes |
  | `completion.ts` | completion semantics (successor seams for `session-complete`) |
  | `events.ts` | session event emission |
  | `moments.ts` | moment extraction (sacred-moment seams) |
  | `naming.ts` | session naming |
  | `migrate.ts` + `migrate-runner.ts` | migration of legacy session shapes |
  | `index.ts` | barrel |
  | `IMPLEMENTATION.md` | the module's own design doc |

  Plus the G1-era siblings in `src/lib/`: `sessionEngine.ts` (master-recommendation
  heuristics: `detectPatterns()` keyword-scans recent reflections for
  restlessness/calm/anger/sadness/clarity; `generateSessionRecommendation()` cascades to
  gentle 10-min breathwork, "Observing Resistance" for anger, 5-min "Just Sit" after 5+
  missed days, 35-min deep inquiry when thriving, else time-of-day defaults;
  `generateMissedDayOutreach()` for 3â€“5 vs 5+ missed days) and `sessionOrchestrator.ts`
  (deterministic per-day planner: `pickPractice()` by gate/dimension/loop/rotation,
  `pickMode()`, `pickDuration()` with energy Ã— gate multipliers, `buildGreeting()`,
  `buildKnowledge()`, `buildPractice()` with intention/closing prompt maps, `buildClosing()`
  with a prediction + next-session teaser; localStorage persistence under
  `orb-sessions-*` / `orb-last-theme` keys).

  ## 4.11 The voice loop (STT/TTS)

  **Files:** `src/machines/voiceMachine.ts`, `src/hooks/useVoiceChat.ts`,
  `tts-speak` / `stt-listen` edge functions.

  `voiceMachine` coordinates mic â‡„ transcription â‡„ playback independent of session state:
  states `idle â‡„ recording â‡„ transcribing â†’ idle` and `idle â‡„ speaking â†’ idle`, with
  `speaking â†’ recording` a first-class transition (barge-in/interrupt-while-speaking).
  `TOGGLE_VOICE` is declared at the top level so it works from any state.

  The loop: `MediaRecorder` captures audio â†’ POST the blob to `stt-listen` (Deepgram) â†’
  `TRANSCRIPTION_DONE` feeds the transcript into the chat; assistant text POSTs to
  `tts-speak` (ElevenLabs) â†’ synthesized audio plays â†’ `SPEAK_DONE`. Errors map to
  `MIC_ERROR`/`TRANSCRIPTION_ERROR`/`SPEAK_ERROR`. This is the "voice orb" experience on
  Home/Guide â€” multi-modal sessions (voice, text, generative) in the README's words.

  ## 4.12 Cinematic practice runs

  **Files:** `src/lib/cinematic/{types,registry}.ts` + `scripts/*`,
  `src/contexts/CinematicSessionContext.tsx`,
  `src/contexts/PracticeRunControllerContext.tsx`, `src/machines/sessionPlayerMachine.ts`,
  `components/cinematic/CinematicSession.tsx`, `SilentModeShell.tsx`.

  A **script-driven, level-adaptive guided-practice engine**, architecturally separate
  from chat:

  - A `CinematicScript` = `{ id, practice, label, defaultDurationSec, surface:
    "black"|"guru-bg"|"starfield", layers: CinematicLayer[] }`.
  - Each **layer (0â€“3)** is a depth of narration: L0 "beginner (most hand-holding)" â†’ L3
    "subtle (least narration, most space)".
  - Each layer is an ordered list of **phases** (`arrival | deepen | checkpoint |
    integrate | close`), each phase an ordered array of **beats** â€” timed subtitle cards
    `{ text, holdMs, silent? }` where `silent: true` marks held-breath/stillness pauses.
  - Mid-phase **checkpoints** ask a single-tap `question` with branching beat arrays
    (`onYes` / `onNotYet`) â€” "no typing", max 3â€“4 per session.
  - `resolveLayer(script, userLevel)` maps a constellation practice level (0â€“7) to a
    layer: â‰¥ 4 â†’ L2, â‰¥ 2 â†’ L1, else L0, capped to authored layers.
  - `getUserPracticeLevel()` reads the level straight from
    `user_practice_constellation` by dynamically keying `` `${practice}_level `` â€” one
    query serves any practice.
  - `pickScript()` resolves by id or practice â†’ `{ script, layerIdx }`.
  - The provider is mounted once in `AppLayout`; `play({ scriptId?, practice?, userId?,
    guruImage?, guruName?, onComplete?, onExit? })` renders the full-screen
    `CinematicSession` overlay **outside the route tree** â€” any surface (Guide chat,
    Practice, Witness) can trigger it and resume its own flow via callbacks.
  - **Silent mode** (`SilentModeShell.tsx`) is the opposite wrapper: strips the UI to
    essentials for practices where visuals would undermine the state (long sits, pure
    witness). Registered alongside cinematic in the `modeRegistry` (Â§8.2).

  The deepest product property of the cinematic system: when a user's practice level
  rises (via `evaluate-practice-level`), the *next* run of the same script silently
  promotes to a sparser narration layer â€” less hand-holding without any settings screen.

  # PART 5 â€” THE BACKEND

  ## 5.1 Supabase architecture

  The backend is **entirely serverless** on Supabase (marketing name: "ConsciousOS Cloud" /
  the AI layer behind an abstraction called the "ConsciousOS AI Gateway"):

  | Service | Usage |
  |---|---|
  | **Postgres** | ~200+ tables, RLS on virtually all of them, `pgvector` for memory embeddings, RPCs (`check_credit_balance`, `decay_memories`, `match_user_memories`, `count_loop_occurrences`, `award_karma_event`, `admin_grant_credits`, â€¦), views, triggers (`handle_new_user`, `update_updated_at_column`, founder auto-grant) |
  | **Auth** | email/OAuth; `auth.users` â†’ `handle_new_user()` trigger seeds `profiles`; client session via `@supabase/supabase-js` |
  | **Edge Functions** | 102 Deno functions (`serve()` from `std/http/server.ts`) â€” all "intelligence" lives here |
  | **Realtime** | per-user channels: `consciousness-bus:{userId}`, notifications, `nudge_queue`, temple sensor layer, admin activity feed |
  | **Storage** | media/assets (manifest-driven, `upload-all-media.mjs` / `generate-media-manifest.mjs` pipelines) |

  ## 5.2 Anatomy of an edge function (the canonical pattern)

  Every AI-calling function follows the same shape (illustrated by `guide-chat`):

  1. **CORS preflight** â€” `OPTIONS` â†’ empty response with shared `corsHeaders`
    (`_shared/cors.ts`).
  2. **Auth extraction** â€” read `Authorization: Bearer â€¦`; construct a **user-scoped
    client** (`createClient(url, ANON_KEY, { headers: { Authorization } })`) so RLS
    applies to reads; `supabase.auth.getUser()` resolves `user.id`; reject 401.
  3. **Admin client** â€” a second service-role client for privileged operations that must
    bypass RLS (credit mutations, cross-user writes).
  4. **Atomic credit pre-check** â€” `admin.rpc("check_credit_balance", { p_user_id,
    p_min_balance: 50 })`; return `402 insufficient_credits` if the user cannot afford
    the call. This is the de facto middleware of the whole backend.
  5. **Do the work** â€” `getUserContext()` read aggregation â†’ engine logic â†’ AI gateway
    calls (Â§5.5) â†’ writes.
  6. **Function-to-function composition** â€” where useful, edge functions call *each
    other* over HTTP with the caller's auth header forwarded (e.g. `guide-chat` â†’
    `session-adapter` on a session's first message) so decisions can be logged and
    evolve on their own release cadence.
  7. **Post-call billing** â€” `computeChargedPaise()` (`_shared/ai-pricing.ts`) from
    token usage; ledger rows in `credit_transactions` + `ai_usage_log` (+ `ai_cost_logs`).

  ## 5.5 The AI gateway and model routing

  All AI calls flow through `_shared/ai-config.ts` (`aiGatewayFetch` /
  `aiGatewayFetchWithLang`) with a **tier** concept and a per-user **aiMode**:

  | Tier / mode | Providers observed | Used for |
  |---|---|---|
  | `tier:nano` / fast-small | Groq `llama-3.1-8b-instant` | cheap extraction, sacred moments, council voices, detection |
  | `tier:fast` | Groq (`llama-3.3-70b-versatile` for synthesis) | loop detection, adaptation decisions, summarization |
  | `tier:main` / premium | OpenAI (and GPT-4/Claude class per platform docs) | session composition, guide chat, teaching generation |
  | Embeddings | Google Gemini `text-embedding-004` | memory vectors, novelty checks, contradiction search |
  | TTS | ElevenLabs | master voices |
  | STT | Deepgram | voice input |

  `identity.aiMode: "fast" | "premium"` (per-user, from the profile) selects the provider
  class for every call a user triggers â€” cost-aware personalization at the routing layer.
  Language injection (`aiGatewayFetchWithLang`, `_shared/language.ts`) localizes AI
  output across the ~40â€“60 supported locales. Every call is logged to `ai_usage_log` with
  token counts and price, aggregated into `ai_cost_logs` (admin cost dashboards), and
  charged via `computeChargedPaise()`.

  ## 5.6 Credits and billing

  - **`user_credits.balance_paise`** â€” balance in paise (â‚¹1 = 100 paise). Every AI call
    costs 10â€“50+ paise by function class.
  - **Pre-check gate**: `check_credit_balance` RPC before any AI work; `402
    insufficient_credits` + client-side `triggerInsufficientCredits()` â†’ credits page.
  - **Ledger**: `credit_transactions` (`bonus | purchase | spend`), e.g. a migration
    backfill granting 1100 free paise to existing users; `admin_grant_credits()` RPC for
    ops grants.
  - **Top-ups**: Razorpay (India-first) â€” `razorpay-order` creates the order,
    `razorpay-verify` verifies the payment signature and credits the balance.
  - **Free tier**: showroom programs (no login) + seeded credits.

  ## 5.7 Security: rate limits, sanitization, crisis gate

  - `_shared/rate-limit.ts` â€” per-user rate limiting on hot functions.
  - `_shared/sanitize-input.ts` â€” input sanitization before prompts.
  - **Crisis gate** â€” `masterMatching.ts` `CRISIS_KEYWORDS` (English + Hinglish) checked
    *before* any spiritual routing; matches return `CRISIS_RESOURCES` (Â§3.8.2).
  - **Prompt-injection surface** â€” master dossiers + adaptive directives are assembled
    server-side; user text never enters system-prompt position (an explicit hardening
    item in the platform roadmap).
  - RLS is the final backstop for everything the client tries to touch directly (Â§6.3).

  ## 5.8 Scheduled / cron workloads

  Cron-invoked functions (service-role / `x-cron-secret`): `pattern-window-detector`
  (30-min), `instant-pulse-detector` (15-min windows), `synthesize-daily-memory`
  (nightly per user), `memory-consolidate` (periodic), `recalibration-intelligence`
  (`all: true` over 30-day-active users), `detect-contradictions` / `detect-cross-
  pollination` / `detect-silence` (nightly), `detect-anomalies` (admin health), 
  `daily-briefing` / `generate-journey-nudge` (re-engagement windows), `org-weekly-
  briefing` (enterprise weekly).

  ## 5.9 Realtime channels

  Used sparingly for live server-push (most data flows through React Query):

  - `useConsciousnessBus.ts` â€” channel `consciousness-bus:{userId}`: engine-side events
    (insight computed, theme evolved, companion noticing) â†’ `ConsciousnessBusListener`
    mounted in `AppLayout` reacts ambiently (toasts, haptics via loop_detected signals).
  - `useNotifications.ts` â€” new `notifications` rows â†’ `NEW_NOTIFICATION` into
    `notificationsMachine` (live bell).
  - `useNudgeQueue.ts` â€” live `nudge_queue` re-engagement nudges.
  - `PracticeInteractiveLayer` (temple sensorial room) and `AdminActivityFeed` (ops
    live tail).

  # PART 6 â€” THE DATABASE

  ## 6.1 Migration history narrative (173 migrations)

  `supabase/migrations/` runs from `20260307160609_*` (March 2026) through
  `20260712042158_*` (July 2026). Reading them in order tells the product's story:

  1. **March 2026 â€” the simple app:** `profiles`, the "core loop" migration
    (`daily_themes`, `sessions`, `session_messages`, `observation_tasks`, `daily_tasks`,
    `journal_entries`, `evening_reflections`), plus `user_consciousness_profile`.
  2. **Early April â€” the engine layer:** loops (`user_loops`, `user_samskaras`,
    `regression_events`), consciousness engine tables (`consciousness_signals`,
    `daily_engine_plans`, `engine_signals`), memory (`guru_memory`,
    `memory_patterns`, `user_memory_vectors`), enterprise core (organizations, teams,
    wellness challenges), billing (`user_credits`, `credit_transactions`, `ai_usage_log`).
  3. **Early April â€” the Temple mythos:** the self-contained
    `20260405000000_temple_schema.sql` (12 tables + 2 views), witness sessions,
    personalized programs, user journeys.
  4. **Aprilâ€“May â€” living themes, assessments, spiritual DNA, karma/XP:** theme arcs,
    assessment engine, `user_spiritual_dna`, `karma_events`/`user_karma`, wearables,
    the workplace-watcher signal layer.
  5. **Mayâ€“July â€” constellation & gamification:** `user_practice_constellation` (+history),
    `practice_level_events`, `v_level_history`, recalibration events, `session_spine`,
    foundation/pre-journey tables, nudge queues, pair invites.

  Many tables are re-declared `IF NOT EXISTS` across files as the schema iterated â€” some
  names exist twice with drift later reconciled at the application layer (e.g. two
  overlapping `user_text_reads` definitions; `practice_sessions_v2` vs `practice_runs`).

  ## 6.2 Domain-by-domain schema

  ### Auth / Profile
  - **`profiles`** â€” `id`, `user_id â†’ auth.users` (UNIQUE, CASCADE), `display_name`,
    `avatar_url`, `bio`, plus later `onboarded`. World-readable SELECT (community/
    leaderboards); owner-only INSERT/UPDATE. `handle_new_user()` trigger seeds from OAuth
    metadata; `update_updated_at_column()` reused by nearly every table.
  - **`user_roles`** â€” role rows (`admin`, `enterprise_admin`, â€¦) consumed by the
    `has_role()` SECURITY DEFINER function, avoiding recursive-RLS.
  - **`user_consciousness_profile`** â€” the core personalization record:
    `overall_band`, `streak_current`, `journey_day`, `assessment_data` jsonb
    (`assigned_guru_id`, `archetype_hint`, â€¦). The row `AppEntry`/`Home` bootstrap from.
  - **`user_psyche_profile`**, **`user_life_stage`**, **`app_relationship_depth`**,
    **`retention_signals`** (new/casual/committed/lapsing), **`feature_flags`**,
    **`data_export_requests`**, audit tables (`admin_activity_log`,
    `impersonation_logs`, `security_audit_log`).

  ### Sessions / Guide
  - **`sessions`** â€” unified: `user_id`, `session_date`, `time_slot`
    (morning|midday|evening|night), `type` (guide|generative|â€¦), `status`
    (active|completed), `theme_id â†’ daily_themes`, `started_at/ended_at`, `summary`
    (JSON â€” session-shell rehydrates plans from here).
  - **`session_messages`** â€” chat transcript; relational RLS via EXISTS-join to
    `sessions` (no direct `user_id` column).
  - **`session_spine`** â€” the v3 durable record: archetype, reason, lead master, council
    voices, primary/secondary loop ids, primary dimension, chakra focus, `why_for_you`
    array, `graph_edges`, `composed_signals`, `quality_score`, `final_user_sentence`.
  - **`session_adaptations`** â€” every session-adapter decision (original vs adapted
    master/mode/practice + reasons + raw signals).
  - `chat_sessions`/`chat_messages`/`guide_messages` â€” legacy chat-history tables from
    earlier Guide iterations.
  - **`session_program_enrollments`**, **`session_program_progress`**,
    **`scheduled_sessions`**, **`daily_sessions`**, **`adaptive_days`**, **`day_chains`**,
    **`day_feedback`**, `session_ingredients` â€” scheduling/adaptive-day planning.

  ### Journal / Memory
  - **`journal_entries`** (`session_id` nullable FK, `title`, `content`, `mood`, `tags`),
    full CRUD RLS including DELETE.
  - **`evening_reflections`** â€” one per `(user_id, reflection_date)`: mood, energy,
    gratitudes, highlights/challenges/lessons.
  - **`sacred_moments`** â€” moment_type (breakthrough|realization|tear|laugh|vow|gratitude|
    grief|first_vulnerability|milestone|life_event|other), title, user_words,
    emotional_tone, importance, occurred_at, source, metadata. â†’ Memory Vault.
  - **`guru_memory`** â€” AI-distilled, importance-scored, layer-classified, decaying
    memories (Â§3.9.1).
  - **`memory_patterns`** â€” cross-session recurring themes (frequency, dimension,
    linked_loops, emotional_trend, significance).
  - **`user_memory_vectors`** â€” pgvector embeddings with source_type/source_id/tags/
    loop_ids; queried by `match_user_memories` / `count_loop_occurrences` RPCs.

  - **`inside_jokes`**, **`future_self_whispers`**, **`higher_self_letters`**,
    **`dream_entries`**, **`legacy_vault`/`legacy_guardians`/`life_events`** â€” narrative

  - **`user_loops`** (personal instances of catalog loops), **`user_samskaras`**,

  **`witness_circles`** + members, **`collective_meditations`** + participants,
  **`community_observations`** + reactions, **`journey_groups`** + members,
  **`peer_circles`** + members + messages (enterprise), `witness_distraction_logs`.

  ### Enterprise
  **`organizations`**, **`teams`**, **`org_members`**, **`org_invites`**,
  **`org_activity_log`**, **`enterprise_notifications`**, **`wellness_challenges`** +
  participants, **`mood_pulses`** (anonymized), **`manager_coaching_notes`**,
  **`org_quarterly_reports`**, **`org_consciousness_snapshots`**, **`org_programs`** +
  participants, **`org_members_directory`** view with explicit grant.

  ### Billing
  **`user_credits`** (paise), **`credit_transactions`**, **`ai_usage_log`**,
  **`ai_cost_logs**, Razorpay flows (Â§5.6).

  ### Admin / ops
  **`prompt_versions`/`prompt_evals`/`training_examples`** (prompt A/B harness),
  **`rate_limits`**, **`broadcasts`**, **`notification_logs`**, **`push_subscriptions`**,
  **`offline_sync_queue`**, **`waitlist_emails`**, **`sales_inquiries`**, founder
  auto-grant trigger.

  ### Karma / gamification
  **`karma_events`**, **`user_karma`**, **`karma_verification_log`**, 
  **`xp_transactions`**, `nudge_queue`.

  ## 6.3 Row-Level Security patterns

  Four patterns cover the whole schema:

  1. **Owner isolation (the default)** â€” virtually every user-owned table:
    ```sql
    ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
    CREATE POLICY "Users view own X"   ON <t> FOR SELECT USING (auth.uid() = user_id);
    CREATE POLICY "Users insert own X" ON <t> FOR INSERT WITH CHECK (auth.uid() = user_id);
    CREATE POLICY "Users update own X" ON <t> FOR UPDATE USING (auth.uid() = user_id);
    ```
    (Deletes added selectively â€” journal, bookmarks, downloads.)
  2. **Relational RLS** â€” child tables lacking `user_id` (e.g. `session_messages`) use
    `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sessions s WHERE s.id = session_messages.session_id AND
    s.user_id = auth.uid())`.
  3. **Globally-readable reference content** â€” `profiles` SELECT, `daily_themes`,
    `assessment_definitions` (content, not user data).
  4. **Role-gated cross-user visibility** â€” admin via `has_role(auth.uid(),'admin')`
    (SECURITY DEFINER to avoid recursion); enterprise manager views via
    org-membership EXISTS predicates (`role IN ('admin','manager')`); one
    view+grant (`org_members_directory`).

  ## 6.4 ER-style relationship summary

    â”‚        â”œâ”€â”€â”€â”€ journal_entries (session_id nullable FK)
    â”‚        â””â”€â”€â”€â”€ session_spine (v3: one per composed generative session)

    â””â”€1:Nâ”€ user_loops, active_loops, regression_events, loop_attributions
    â””â”€1:Nâ”€ temple_journeys â”€â”€(master_id)â”€â”€ [static master catalog in src/lib]
    â””â”€1:Nâ”€ temple_journal, temple_presences, temple_calendar_events, temple_downloads
    â””â”€1:Nâ”€ credit_transactions â”€â”€aggregatesâ”€â”€> user_credits.balance_paise

# CONSCIOUSOS â€” PLATFORM OVERVIEW
## Complete Architecture, Engines, Innovations & Capabilities
### For Investors, Board Members & Strategic Partners

# WHAT THIS DOCUMENT COVERS







This document explains, in plain English, what ConsciousOS has built â€” from the overall system architecture down to the specific innovations that make us unique. It is organized into five sections:

1. **The Big Picture** â€” How the platform is structured and why it works the way it does
2. **The Engine Room** â€” The proprietary intelligence layer that powers every decision
3. **The Framework** â€” The consciousness model that underpins everything
4. **The User Experience** â€” What a user actually sees and does, from first open to daily use
5. **The Innovation Inventory** â€” Every major feature, system, and capability we've built

The goal is to give you a complete mental model of the platform without requiring a technical background. If you can understand this document, you understand the business.

---







# PART 1 â€” THE BIG PICTURE







## How the Platform Is Organized

ConsciousOS is built in four layers, each depending on the one below it:









## Layer 1 â€” The Consciousness Model (The "What")









This is our proprietary map of human psychological and spiritual development. It is the foundation everything else sits on. It includes:

- **11 Levels of Development** â€” From "I am my thoughts" to "only one exists"
- **96 Dimensions of Awareness** â€” Granular maps of thought, emotion, body, action, connection, and spirit
- **300+ Behavioral Patterns** â€” Catalogued psychological loops with root-cause analysis
- **6 Life Domains** â€” Relationships, work, health, finance, creativity, spirituality
- **8 Hidden Psychological Stages** â€” Internal markers that guide how we interact with users (never shown to them)

This is not a wellness framework borrowed from someone else. This is original work, developed over years, with specific mathematical relationships between the levels, dimensions, and patterns.









## Layer 2 â€” The Engine Layer (The "How")









This is the proprietary software that runs the consciousness model. It consists of six interconnected engines:

- **The V-Level Engine** â€” Computes where a user is in their development right now
- **The Practice Engine** â€” Decides what practice the user needs next
- **The Adaptive Engine** â€” Reconfigures the AI's behavior for every single interaction
- **The Retention Engine** â€” Detects when a user is disengaging and prescribes a re-engagement gesture
- **The Speed Profile Engine** â€” Learns how fast a user practices and adjusts difficulty accordingly
- **The Dimension Tracker** â€” Records how each practice affects the user's dimensions over time

These engines run on the server (in our edge functions) and in the user's device (in the app) simultaneously. The server is the source of truth; the client enables instant responses.









## Layer 3 â€” The Content Layer (The "What You Do")









This is the library of experiences the user can engage with:

- **270+ Interactive Games** â€” Not videos. Not guided meditations. Interactive exercises where the user does something (write, reflect, choose, breathe, move)
- **120+ Structured Programs** â€” Multi-day journeys organized by theme (shadow work, emotional regulation, purpose, relationships, etc.)
- **25+ Authentic Practices** â€” Drawn from world traditions (Ramana Maharshi, Patanjali, Buddha, Krishna, Thich Nhat Hanh, etc.)
- **50+ Wisdom Cards** â€” Cross-tradition wisdom with neuroscience commentary
- **8-Band Stage Handbook** â€” For each development level: what to practice, what to avoid, what to watch for

The content is selected for the user by the engines. Two users at the same level may get completely different experiences based on their dimensions, loops, life domains, and current state.









## Layer 4 â€” The Interface Layer (The "What You See")









This is the mobile app, web app, and enterprise dashboard the user interacts with:

- **Mobile App** â€” iOS and Android (built with Capacitor)
- **Web App** â€” Progressive web app with offline capability
- **Enterprise Dashboard** â€” For organizations to manage wellness programs
- **Admin Panel** â€” For internal operations

The interface is designed to be minimal, calming, and time-appropriate. Morning screens feel different from evening screens. The app adapts its visual language to the user's state.

---







# PART 2 â€” THE ENGINE ROOM







## What Is an Engine?

In our system, an "engine" is a piece of proprietary software that makes a specific type of decision. Think of them as specialized organs in a body â€” each does one job, but together they produce intelligence.









## Engine 1: The V-Level Engine










This is the most important engine. It answers one question: **Where is this user in their development right now?**









## How It Works (Simplified)

Every user has six dimension scores (thought, emotion, body, action, connection, spirit) that range from 0 to 10. The V-Level Engine blends these six numbers using a specific mathematical formula:

- It takes the geometric mean of the four primary dimensions (thought, emotion, body, spirit)
- It takes the harmonic mean
- It takes the minimum and maximum
- It blends them: 40% geometric, 30% harmonic, 20% minimum, 10% maximum
- It multiplies by an "integration factor" based on action and connection
- It adjusts for witness score, active loops, and integration bonus
- It clamps the result for new users (first 30 days)

The output is a single number: the V-Level (0.00 to 10.00). The integer part is the Band (0-10). The decimal part is the micro-level (entering, deepening, mastery).









## Why It Matters

The V-Level is the backbone of the entire system. It determines:
- What content the user sees
- What practices they're ready for
- How the AI speaks to them
- What their progression path looks like
- When to introduce more advanced concepts

No other wellness product has this kind of granular, mathematically-defined progression model.









## Engine 2: The Practice Engine










This engine answers: **What should the user do right now?**









## How It Works (Simplified)

The Practice Engine scores every available practice against the user's current state. It uses eight weighted rules:

1. **Weakest core practice** â€” Target the dimension that needs the most work (+10 points)
2. **Anchor path** â€” Reinforce the practice that's already working (+5 points)
3. **Growth path** â€” If the anchor is plateauing, push to the next level (+7 points)
4. **Active loops** â€” If the user has a behavioral pattern (e.g., validation-seeking), prioritize practices that dissolve that loop (+8 points per loop)
5. **Life domain stress** â€” If work is stressful, prioritize practices that target the relevant dimension (+6 points per stressed domain)
6. **Cross-pollination** â€” If a practice is plateaued, introduce something complementary (+4 points)
7. **Weakest dimension** â€” Bonus for practices that strongly target the weakest dimension (+3 points)
8. **Specialty gating** â€” Some practices are locked until the user reaches a certain level (e.g., advanced energy practices require Band 4+)

The practice with the highest score wins. If all scores are zero (edge case), the system defaults to a foundational practice.









## Why It Matters

This is the personalization engine. It's what makes the experience feel "right" for each user. The weights are proprietary, and the combination of all eight rules is unique to ConsciousOS.









## Engine 3: The Adaptive Engine










This is the most technically innovative engine. It answers: **How should the AI behave in this specific moment?**









## How It Works (Simplified)

Every time a user sends a message or completes a practice, the Adaptive Engine:

1. **Reads the user's state** â€” energy, emotion, awareness, resistance, clarity, intent, fragility
2. **Detects edge cases** â€” Is the user spiritual bypassing? Over-dependent? In an intellectual loop? In crisis?
3. **Determines hidden stage** â€” Assigns a stage 0-7 that is never shown to the user but guides AI behavior
4. **Routes cognitive agents** â€” Selects 2-3 AI "personas" for this turn (companion, pattern-finder, witness-keeper, etc.)
5. **Sets insight depth** â€” How much framework language to use (micro, framework, explicit, none)
6. **Sets reveal level** â€” How much Sanskrit or technical terminology (1-4)
7. **Generates a directive** â€” A formatted instruction block injected into the AI's system prompt

This directive changes every turn. It means the AI is not a static chatbot with a fixed personality â€” it's a dynamic system that meets the user exactly where they are.









## Why It Matters

This is the "secret sauce" of the product. No competitor has anything like it. It's what makes the AI feel intelligent, present, and responsive â€” not like a pre-programmed bot.









## Engine 4: The Retention Engine










This engine answers: **Is this user disengaging, and what should we do about it?**









## How It Works (Simplified)

The Retention Engine looks at four signals:
- Session rate (how often they practice)
- Average quality (how present they are)
- Streak days (consecutive days of practice)
- Engagement level (depth of interaction)

It blends these into a retention score and classifies the user into one of five risk levels:

| Risk Level | Prescription | What It Means |
|------------|-------------|---------------|
| **Thriving** | Silent celebration | User is engaged and progressing. No intervention needed. |
| **Stable** | Micro pulse | User is steady. Send a tiny, non-intrusive notification. |
| **Drifting** | Gentle nudge | User is slipping. Send a soft re-engagement message. |
| **Disengaged** | Reflect invite | User is absent. Invite them to journal or reflect. |
| **Lost** | Arya returns | User is gone. Full re-onboarding experience. |









## Why It Matters

Most wellness apps panic when users disengage and spam them with notifications. This makes users feel guilty and increases churn. Our engine prescribes graduated, contextually appropriate gestures that respect the user's state.









## Engine 5: The Speed Profile Engine










This engine answers: **How fast does this user practice, and what difficulty level is right for them?**









## How It Works (Simplified)

The engine classifies users into three speed profiles:
- **Fast** â€” Burns through practices quickly, high quality
- **Steady** â€” Consistent pace, moderate quality
- **Deep** â€” Slow, deliberate, high depth

It then adjusts difficulty based on the last three practice quality scores:
- If quality is consistently high â†’ increase difficulty
- If quality is consistently low â†’ decrease difficulty
- If quality is steady â†’ stabilize

It also detects guilt patterns (apologetic language, long gaps, shortened sessions) and responds with anti-guilt messaging and a suggested 2-minute restart practice.









## Why It Matters

Most apps use a one-size-fits-all difficulty curve. This engine adapts to the user's natural pace, reducing frustration for slow practitioners and boredom for fast ones.









## Engine 6: The Dimension Tracker










This engine answers: **How did this practice change the user's consciousness state?**









## How It Works (Simplified)

When a user completes a practice, the Dimension Tracker:
1. Looks up the practice's impact on each dimension (pre-defined in a matrix)
2. Calculates a "delta" (change) for each affected dimension based on:
   - The practice's base impact
   - The user's self-rated quality
   - The actual duration practiced
   - The practice level
3. Applies the delta to the user's dimension scores using an exponential moving average (smoothing)
4. Detects trends (rising, stable, falling) for each dimension
5. Feeds the updated scores back to the V-Level Engine







## Why It Matters

This is the feedback loop that makes the system learn. Every practice changes the user's state, and the Dimension Tracker records that change. Over time, the system builds a detailed map of how this specific user responds to different practices.

---







# PART 3 â€” THE FRAMEWORK







## What Is the Consciousness Model?

The consciousness model is our proprietary map of human development. It is the "theory of the user" that all our engines operate on. It has five components:









## Component 1: Bands (11 Levels)









The user progresses through 11 levels, from "The Sleeper" (Band 0: "I am my thoughts") to "The Absolute" (Band 10: "Only one exists"). Each band has three micro-levels (entering, deepening, mastery), giving 33 addressable sub-stages.

| Band | Name | Core Characteristic |
|------|------|---------------------|
| 0 | The Sleeper | Identified with thoughts, no awareness of awareness |
| 1 | The Questioner | Beginning to wonder if thoughts are not-self |
| 2 | The Observer | Can observe thoughts without being caught in them |
| 3 | The Alchemist | Pain becomes teacher; transformation begins |
| 4 | The Navigator | Can choose responses instead of reacting |
| 5 | The Architect | Co-creates reality; intentional living |
| 6 | The Channel | Life lives through them; surrender deepens |
| 7 | The Rishi | 24/7 awareness; turiya (beyond normal states) |
| 8 | The Jagadguru | Universal compassion; others' suffering matters as own |
| 9 | The Avatar | Cosmic consciousness; non-dual awareness |
| 10 | The Absolute | Shunya; only one exists |

Bands 0-6 are the "human journey" â€” the levels most users will reach. Bands 7-10 are transpersonal â€” available but rare.









## Component 2: Dimensions (96 Sub-Dimensions)









The user's consciousness is mapped across six primary dimensions, each with 16 sub-dimensions:

**Thought:** Clarity, belief patterns, inner critic, mental loops, decision style, memory use, attention span, cognitive bias, mind wandering, self-talk, curiosity, skepticism, worry index, planning, perspective-taking, meta-cognition

**Emotion:** Range, regulation, awareness, expression, empathy, joy, grief, anger, fear, shame, compassion, vulnerability, boundaries, mood stability, emotional honesty, heart openness

**Body:** Energy, sleep, tension, breath, posture, hunger cues, movement, rest quality, sensory awareness, nervous system tone, gut signals, sexual energy, pain awareness, vitality, embodiment, somatic memory

**Action:** Integrity, discipline, procrastination, follow-through, risk, patience, service, effort, habit strength, recovery, conflict style, generosity, reactivity, initiative, courage, restraint

**Connection:** Intimacy, trust, listening, repair, belonging, solitude tolerance, family bond, friendship depth, romantic bond, community, conflict repair, forgiveness, receiving love, giving love, witnessing others, being seen

**Spirit:** Witness, surrender, devotion, awe, silence, non-dual glimpses, faith, mystery, death awareness, cosmic sense, ego loosening, presence, meaning, sacred sense, inner stillness, transcendence

The system tracks all 96 sub-dimensions, but the engine math operates on the six primary dimensions.









## Component 3: Loops (300+ Patterns)









A "loop" is a behavioral or psychological pattern that repeats â€” often unconsciously. The system has catalogued 300+ loops across 27 categories:

**Categories:** Validation-seeking, control, avoidance, attachment, comparison, narrative, survival, body, energy, emotional, mental, behavioral, habit, social, relationship, identity, ego, belief, cognitive bias, attention, motivation, decision, learning, productivity, creativity, purpose, spiritual

Each loop has:
- A name (e.g., "The Approval Hunger" for validation-seeking)
- A root wound (e.g., abandonment, powerlessness, perfectionism)
- A chakra correlation
- Symptoms
- A dissolution path (how to break it)









## Component 4: Life Domains (6 Areas)









The user's wellbeing is tracked across six life domains:

- **Relationship** â€” Intimacy, family, friendship, community
- **Work** â€” Career, purpose, fulfillment, burnout
- **Finance** â€” Security, abundance, scarcity, money mindset
- **Health** â€” Physical wellbeing, chronic conditions, energy
- **Creativity** â€” Expression, flow, artistic practice
- **Spirituality** â€” Practice, meaning, connection, awe

Life domains are separate from dimensions. Dimensions track consciousness faculties; life domains track real-world arenas. They interact: when a life domain is stressed, the Practice Engine boosts practices that target the relevant dimension.









## Component 5: The Hidden Stage Model (8 Stages)









The system assigns every user a "stage" from 0-7 based on their awareness, reactivity, pattern recognition, self-inquiry, and consistency. This stage is NEVER shown to the user. It only drives AI behavior â€” determining how the AI speaks, what it suggests, and how much it holds space.

| Stage | Name | AI Behavior |
|-------|------|-------------|
| 0 | Unaware | High structure, maximum guidance |
| 1 | Beginning to notice | Gentle introductions to awareness |
| 2 | Observer emerging | Encouragement of witnessing practice |
| 3 | Consistent witness | Deeper inquiry, less hand-holding |
| 4 | Self-inquiry active | Non-directive, question-based |
| 5 | Non-dual glimpses | Minimal guidance, spacious presence |
| 6 | Stabilized awareness | Almost no intervention |
| 7 | Turiya / 24/7 | Complete silence; no AI presence |

This is one of the most sophisticated parts of the system. The AI's behavior is not a fixed personality â€” it morphs based on the user's hidden stage.

---







# PART 4 â€” THE SESSION FLOW







## What Is a Session?

A session is the core user experience. It is a structured, time-bound practice that typically lasts 15, 30, or 60 minutes. Every session has four phases:









## Phase 1: Teaching (30% of session)









The user's guide/master introduces the theme for the day. This is not a lecture â€” it's a short, pointed teaching (2-5 minutes) that sets up the practice.

Example: "Today's theme is Surrender in Action. The teaching is about the difference between effort and allowing. Notice how you approach your work today â€” are you forcing, or are you allowing?"









## Phase 2: Practice (50% of session)









The user engages with an interactive practice selected by the Practice Engine. This is not a passive experience â€” the user is doing something:

- **Breath practices:** Coherent breathing, box breath, alternate nostril
- **Body practices:** Body scan, tension release, movement awareness
- **Self-inquiry practices:** "Who am I?", Neti Neti, self-reflection
- **Witness practices:** Observing thoughts, emotions, sensations without reacting
- **Emotional practices:** Grief work, anger processing, joy cultivation
- **Shadow practices:** Interviewing the shadow, ego autopsy
- **Relationship practices:** Two chairs, mirror work, forgiveness
- **Decision practices:** Dharma decision protocol, svadharma (right action)
- **Silence practices:** Timed silence, solitude, mauna (speechlessness)
- **Three brains:** Head-heart-gut integration exercises

Each practice is a game component â€” an interactive React component with its own logic, visuals, and feedback.









## Phase 3: Reflection (15% of session)









After the practice, the user is invited to reflect:

- **Chat interface:** "What arose for you in the practice?" The AI responds based on the Adaptive Engine directive
- **Journal prompt:** "Write about what you noticed."
- **Rating:** "How present were you?" (0-100%)









## Phase 4: Integration (5% of session)









The session closes with:

- An action item for the day (e.g., "Notice one moment of effort today")
- A micro-practice for later (e.g., "Three breaths before your next meeting")
- A preview of the next session







## How a Session Is Composed










When the user taps "Begin Session," the following happens in sequence:









## Step 1: The Brain Snapshot










The system builds a complete picture of the user's current state by querying the database for:
- Current band and V-Level
- Six dimension scores
- Active loops and samskaras
- Life domain scores
- Spiritual DNA (practice preferences)
- Practice constellation (mastery levels)
- Recent session history
- Active theme arc

This is called "The Brain" â€” a single JSON object that contains everything the engines need to make decisions.









## Step 2: Consciousness Engine Processing










The Brain is fed into the consciousness engine, which:
- Recalculates V-Level with latest data
- Detects state (dark night, bypassing, plateau, breakthrough, normal)
- Computes bypassing risk
- Identifies archetype
- Applies time decay if the user has been away







## Step 3: Practice Selection










The Practice Engine scores all available practices against the user's state and selects the highest-scoring one. It considers:
- Weakest dimension
- Active loops
- Life domain stress
- Current band (gating)
- Plateau status







## Step 4: Master Selection










The system selects which master/guide will speak to the user. This is based on:
- Active loops (some masters specialize in specific loops)
- Life domain stress (some masters specialize in work, relationships, etc.)
- Time of day
- Rotation rules (so the user doesn't get the same master every day)

There are 30+ masters, including both fictional Sanskrit-named personas and public-domain historical figures (Socrates, Gibran, etc.).









## Step 5: Theme Selection










The system selects or generates a theme for the day, considering:
- Active theme arc (7/21/49-day journey)
- Current phase of the arc
- Lunar phase (new moon, full moon, waxing, waning)
- Season
- User's current state







## Step 6: Session Composition










The metrics engine assembles the SessionPlan:
- Teaching content (from tradition packs or AI-generated)
- Practice selection (from the 270+ game components)
- Reflection prompt (from templates or AI-generated)
- Integration action item
- Cinematic directive (color, mood, visual)

The 30/50/20 formula is applied: 30% teaching, 50% practice, 20% reflection.









## Step 7: AI Content Generation










For dynamic content, the system calls the LLM (GPT-4 or Claude) with:
- The base system prompt (master persona)
- The adaptive directive (per-turn AI behavior)
- The session context (theme, practices, arc)
- The user context (band, loops, dimensions)

The LLM generates the teaching text, practice narration, and reflection prompts.









## Step 8: Adaptive Directive Generation










A separate directive is generated for the AI's behavior during the session's chat/reflection phase. This ensures the AI's tone, depth, and approach are perfectly matched to the user's current state.









## Step 9: Delivery to Client










The complete SessionPlan + Directive is sent to the app. The session machine (XState) takes over and guides the user through the four phases.

---







# PART 5 â€” THE INNOVATION INVENTORY







## What We've Built That No One Else Has







## 1. The Consciousness Model










**What it is:** A proprietary, mathematically-defined map of human psychological and spiritual development.

**What it includes:**
- 11 development levels with micro-stages
- 96 dimensions of awareness
- 300+ behavioral patterns with root-cause analysis
- 8 hidden psychological stages
- Specific mathematical relationships between all components

**Why it matters:** This is the "theory of the user" that powers every decision in the system. No competitor has anything close. Headspace has 10 practices. We have a map of the entire territory.

**Defensibility:** HIGH â€” requires years of curation and domain expertise. The specific mathematical formulations are original.









## 2. The Adaptive Engine (Per-Turn AI Directive)









**What it is:** A system that generates a fresh AI system prompt for every single interaction, based on the user's real-time psychological state.

**What it does:**
- Reads the user's state from their message, time, session history, and current band
- Detects edge cases (spiritual bypassing, over-dependency, crisis, etc.)
- Routes 2-3 cognitive "agents" (personas) for this turn
- Sets insight depth and reveal level
- Generates a formatted directive block injected into the AI's system prompt

**Why it matters:** This makes the AI feel genuinely intelligent and present. It's not a chatbot with a fixed personality â€” it's a dynamic system that adapts to where the user is in that moment.

**Defensibility:** VERY HIGH â€” this is a novel architecture that requires integration with the entire engine stack.









## 3. Graceful Disappearance










**What it is:** A design pattern where the AI intentionally reduces its presence as the user matures.

**How it works:**
- Band 0-2: High AI presence â€” maximum guidance and structure
- Band 3-5: Moderate presence â€” occasional check-ins, suggestions
- Band 6-7: Minimal presence â€” silent tracking, occasional nudges
- Band 8-10: No AI presence â€” the user has internalized the system

**Why it matters:** This is the "anti-app" thesis in action. Every other wellness app optimizes for retention. We optimize for liberation. The measure of success is not how much the user uses the app â€” it's how little they need it.

**Defensibility:** VERY HIGH â€” this is a counter-intuitive insight that requires full user lifecycle data and a genuine commitment to the philosophy.









## 4. The Practice Engine (Additive Weighted Scoring)









**What it is:** A decision engine that selects the optimal practice from 270+ options using eight weighted rules.

**How it works:**
- Each candidate practice is scored based on weakest dimension, active loops, life domain stress, plateau status, specialty gating, and more
- The highest-scoring practice wins
- If all scores are zero, a foundational practice is selected as fallback

**Why it matters:** This is the personalization engine. It makes the experience feel tailor-made for each user. The specific weight combinations and rule interactions are proprietary.

**Defensibility:** HIGH â€” the scoring logic is original and the practice library is massive.









## 5. The V-Level Calculator










**What it is:** A psychometric engine that computes a composite score from six dimensions using a specific mathematical blend.

**How it works:**
- 40% geometric mean of primary dimensions
- 30% harmonic mean
- 20% minimum
- 10% maximum
- Multiplied by integration factor (action + connection)
- Adjusted for witness score, loops, and integration bonus

**Why it matters:** This is the progression model. It determines where the user is and what they're ready for. The specific formula is original and has no known equivalent in any wellness product.

**Defensibility:** MEDIUM-HIGH â€” the algorithm can be reverse-engineered, but the validation data and specific calibration make it defensible.









## 6. Signal Fusion Architecture










**What it is:** A system that integrates multiple external signals into a unified user state model.

**Signals integrated:**
- Wearables (HRV, sleep, steps, heart rate)
- Calendar (meeting load, time of day)
- Email (Gmail, Outlook tone analysis)
- Chat (Slack, Teams sentiment)
- Development tools (GitHub late-night patterns)
- Task management (Linear workload)

**How it works:** Each signal is processed by a dedicated "watcher" function. The signal-fusion engine merges them into a unified UserSignal object that feeds into the Adaptive Engine, Practice Engine, and Retention Engine.

**Why it matters:** This makes the system multi-modal â€” it doesn't just rely on what the user types. It reads their physiology, their schedule, their communication patterns, and their work habits.

**Defensibility:** HIGH â€” the specific fusion formulas and integration patterns are original.









## 7. The Loop Taxonomy










**What it is:** A catalogue of 300+ behavioral and psychological patterns, each with a root-wound analysis, chakra correlation, and dissolution path.

**Structure:**
- 27 categories (validation, control, avoidance, attachment, comparison, etc.)
- Each loop has: name, category, root wound, chakra, symptoms, dissolution path
- Loops are tagged to practices that dissolve them
- The system tracks loop status (active, healing, resolved, transcended)

**Why it matters:** This is the "diagnostic" layer of the system. It's what allows the Practice Engine to say "you're in a validation-seeking loop, here's a practice that helps." No competitor has this depth of pattern cataloguing.

**Defensibility:** MEDIUM â€” the concept of behavioral loops is public domain, but the specific 300+ catalogue with root-wound mappings is original curation.









## 8. The 270+ Game Components










**What it is:** A library of interactive practice experiences, each implemented as a standalone React component.

**Examples:**
- **Witness Room:** A virtual space where you observe thoughts passing by
- **Two Chairs:** A dialogue between two perspectives on a decision
- **Grief Wave:** An interactive emotional processing exercise
- **Shadow Interview:** A guided conversation with your shadow self
- **Three Brains:** Head-heart-gut integration exercise
- **Dharma Yuddha:** An Arjuna-style decision battlefield
- **Bardo Room:** A threshold consciousness experience
- **The Apology:** A guided forgiveness practice

**Why it matters:** This is the largest library of interactive consciousness exercises ever built. Most wellness apps have 10-50 practices. We have 270+. And each one is interactive â€” not a video you watch, but an exercise you do.

**Defensibility:** MEDIUM â€” practice concepts are public domain, but the specific implementations and interactive designs are original.









## 9. The Hidden Stage Model










**What it is:** An 8-stage psychological development model that drives AI behavior but is never shown to the user.

**How it works:**
- The system assigns a stage 0-7 based on awareness, reactivity, pattern recognition, self-inquiry, and consistency
- The stage is smoothed (70% previous + 30% current, capped at Â±1 per turn)
- The stage determines AI behavior: tone, depth, guidance level, silence tolerance
- The user never sees their stage number

**Why it matters:** This is what makes the AI feel like it "gets" the user. A user at stage 2 (observer emerging) gets encouragement and gentle guidance. A user at stage 5 (non-dual glimpses) gets spacious, minimal, question-based interaction. The AI morphs its personality without the user knowing why.

**Defensibility:** VERY HIGH â€” this is a novel application of developmental psychology to AI behavior.









## 10. Multi-Modal Signal Integration










**What it is:** The ability to read and integrate data from wearables, calendar, email, chat, and development tools into the user's consciousness model.

**Signals:**
- **Wearables:** HRV, sleep quality, steps, heart rate, activity
- **Calendar:** Meeting load, back-to-back density, time of day
- **Email:** Sentiment analysis, tone, response patterns
- **Chat:** Slack/Teams sentiment, communication style
- **Development tools:** Late-night coding, commit frequency, workload

**Why it matters:** Most wellness apps rely solely on self-reported data. We have objective, continuous physiological and behavioral data. This allows us to detect patterns the user isn't aware of (e.g., "Your HRV drops after meetings â€” let's add a post-meeting micro-practice").

**Defensibility:** HIGH â€” the integration patterns and fusion formulas are original.









## 11. The 120+ Program Library










**What it is:** Structured, multi-day practice programs organized into 10 categories.

**Categories:**
- Awareness, Mind, Emotional, Body, Meditation, Loops, Yoga Paths, Habits, Purpose, Relationships, Shadow

**Structure:**
- Each program has a defined duration (7, 21, 40, 100 days)
- Each day has teaching, practice, reflection, and integration
- Programs are tagged by difficulty, prerequisites, and audience

**Why it matters:** This provides structured pathways for users who want more guidance than the adaptive engine provides. It's the "curriculum" layer of the platform.

**Defensibility:** MEDIUM â€” program structures are common, but the specific content and integration with the consciousness model is original.









## 12. The Stage Handbook










**What it is:** A detailed guide for each of the 11 bands, specifying:
- The user's identity at this stage
- 30+ markers (how to recognize this stage)
- Inner experience description
- Yoga path fit
- Start-here practices
- Common traps
- Bridge to next band

**Why it matters:** This is the "user manual" for the consciousness model. It's what allows the system to give precise, stage-appropriate guidance. No competitor has anything like this level of developmental mapping.

**Defensibility:** HIGH â€” requires deep expertise in consciousness development and yoga psychology.









## 13. The Enterprise Module










**What it is:** A complete B2B wellness platform built into the same system.

**Capabilities:**
- Organization management (orgs, teams, roles)
- Wellness challenges (time-bound, metric-driven)
- Consciousness snapshots (aggregated, anonymized)
- Quarterly reports (PDF-ready)
- People intelligence (manager coaching, team dynamics)

**Why it matters:** Enterprise wellness is a $60B market. Most competitors are B2C-only. We have a full B2B module ready to pilot.

**Defensibility:** MEDIUM â€” enterprise features are common, but the consciousness engine integration is unique.









## 14. The Memory System










**What it is:** A long-term memory system that remembers user breakthroughs, patterns, and significant moments.

**Components:**
- **Sacred Moments:** Significant experiences flagged by the user or detected by the system
- **Memory Vectors:** Vector embeddings of memories for semantic recall
- **Daily Synthesis:** AI-generated daily summaries
- **Inside Jokes:** AI-detected humor and personal references
- **Future Self Whispers:** Messages from the user's future self
- **Higher Self Letters:** Letters from the user's higher self
- **Dream Journal:** Dream recording and analysis
- **Legacy Vault:** Long-term aspirations and legacy documents

**Why it matters:** This creates a sense of continuity and relationship. The app "remembers" the user's journey, their breakthroughs, their patterns. Most apps have no memory beyond the last session.

**Defensibility:** HIGH â€” the specific memory types and their integration with the consciousness model are original.









## 15. The Journal Thread System










**What it is:** A structured journaling system that goes beyond free-form writing.

**Features:**
- Thread-based journaling (conversation with your past self)
- AI-guided prompts based on current state
- Emotional arc tracking
- Pattern detection across entries
- Integration with practice sessions
- Evening reflection prompts
- Morning intention setting

**Why it matters:** Journaling is a core consciousness practice, but most apps treat it as an afterthought. We've built it as a first-class citizen with AI guidance and pattern detection.

**Defensibility:** MEDIUM â€” journaling is common, but the AI-guided, state-adaptive, thread-based approach is original.

# PART 6 â€” THE USER EXPERIENCE







## First Contact: The Showroom







Before a user signs up, they can experience the platform through the Showroom â€” 7-day free programs with no login required. These are our conversion funnel:

- **Decision-Making with Krishna** â€” For founders and leaders
- **The Art of Letting Go** â€” For people dealing with attachment
- **Morning Rituals** â€” For people wanting structure
- **Emotional Resilience** â€” For people dealing with anxiety
- **The Observer's Path** â€” For people interested in self-inquiry
- And 25+ more, each tagged for specific audiences

The Showroom demonstrates the quality of our content and the sophistication of our engine without requiring commitment.









## Onboarding: The Pre-Journey Flow







When a user signs up, they go through a structured onboarding designed to build their initial consciousness profile:









## Step 1: Quick Questions (4 questions)









These questions assess the user's:
- Belief systems (35% weight)
- Emotional response patterns (30% weight)
- Practice history (20% weight)
- Intent (15% weight)

Based on the answers, the system assigns an initial Band (0-6.9) and recommends a starting path.









## Step 2: Path Selection










The user chooses a yoga path or practice orientation:
- **Bhakti** (devotion)
- **Kriya** (energy/body)
- **Jnana** (knowledge/inquiry)
- **Karma** (action/service)
- **Tantra** (embodiment)
- **Raja** (meditation/mental discipline)

This shapes the tone and content of their journey.









## Step 3: Loop Identification










The user swipes through 20-30 loop cards, identifying which patterns resonate. This builds their initial loop profile.









## Step 4: Lifestyle Personalization










The user sets:
- Preferred practice times (morning, midday, evening, night)
- Available session lengths (5, 15, 30, 60 minutes)
- Notification preferences
- Energy profile (morning person, night owl)









## Step 5: Goals










The user sets 1-3 intentions for their journey. These are used to personalize content and track progress.









## Step 6: Pre-Journey Gate










A time-appropriate gate that transitions the user into their first session. This includes:
- Philosophy prologue (the "anti-app" explanation)
- Foundation introduction
- Final assessment







## Daily Use: The Time Gates







The app is organized around four time gates, each with its own character:

**Morning Gate (6am-12pm):**
- Theme: New beginnings, intention setting
- Practices: Breath, movement, energizing
- AI tone: Fresh, encouraging, clear

**Midday Gate (12pm-5pm):**
- Theme: Maintenance, awareness in action
- Practices: Micro-practices (60 seconds), body awareness
- AI tone: Light, supportive, non-intrusive

**Evening Gate (5pm-9pm):**
- Theme: Integration, reflection, release
- Practices: Longer sessions, emotional processing, journaling
- AI tone: Deeper, more spacious

**Night Sanctuary (9pm-6am):**
- Theme: Rest, surrender, dream
- Practices: Body scan, yoga nidra, dream journal
- AI tone: Minimal, soft, almost silent







## The Home Screen







The home screen is the user's daily dashboard. It shows:
- **Time slot cards** â€” Available practices for morning, midday, evening, night
- **Companion orb** â€” An animated visual presence that reflects the AI's state
- **Today's theme** â€” The day's teaching focus
- **Quick actions** â€” Practice, Journal, Programs, Temple, Community
- **Nudge queue** â€” Gentle, time-appropriate notifications

The home screen is designed to feel calm and inviting â€” not a list of tasks to complete.









## The Session Screen







When the user taps a time slot, they enter the session machine (XState). The session has four phases:

**Teaching Phase:**
- Master appears (avatar + name)
- Theme is introduced (2-5 minute audio + text)
- User can listen or skip ahead

**Practice Phase:**
- Game component loads (270+ options)
- TTS narration plays (if enabled)
- Timer counts down
- User interacts with the practice
- Voice orb animates in sync

**Reflection Phase:**
- Chat interface opens
- AI asks: "What arose for you?"
- User types or speaks
- AI responds based on Adaptive Directive

**Integration Phase:**
- Action item for the day
- Micro-practice suggestion
- Preview of next session
- XP/karma awarded







## The Practice Tab (Sutra)







The Practice tab is separate from the Session tab. It's for users who want to choose their own practice rather than follow a composed session. The Sutra engine:
- Reads the user's practice constellation (12-practice mastery map)
- Shows eligible practices (unlocked based on band and level)
- Recommends based on weakest dimension and current state
- Tracks practice history and level progression







## The Journal







The journal is a structured reflection system:

- **AI-guided prompts:** Based on current state and active loops
- **Emotional arc:** Tracks emotional patterns over time

- **Morning intentions:** Prompts for day-starting

The analytics layer provides deep visualization of the user's consciousness state:

- **Consciousness Loom:** A radial visualization of the 96 dimensions
- **Radar Chart:** Six-dimension spider chart showing balance/imbalance
- **Council of Yogis:** Multi-master perspective on progress
- **Spiritual DNA Panel:** Practice preferences and paths
- **Life Domain Panel:** Six life domain scores over time
- **Dimension Human Map:** Body-map visualization of dimension scores
- **Chakras Layer:** Chakra balance visualization
- **Patterns Layer:** Active loops and their trajectories
- **Predictive Insights:** ML-driven predictions (breakthrough imminent, churn risk, etc.)
- **Weekly Mirror:** Weekly synthesis and reflection prompt







## The Programs Tab







Users can browse and enroll in structured programs:

- **120+ programs** across 11 categories
- **Filterable** by difficulty, duration, category
- **Progress tracked** day by day
- **Community features** (some programs have group enrollment)
- **Temple programs** â€” Sacred-space themed programs with deeper content







## The Temple







The Temple is the app's deeper layer â€” a sacred-space metaphor for advanced practices:

- **Temple Journeys:** Multi-day themed journeys with daily practices
- **Temple Programs:** Structured programs within the temple

# PART 7 â€” THE TECHNOLOGY (NON-TECHNICAL)









## What Technology Powers This (And Why It Matters)









## The Backend










The backend is entirely serverless â€” no traditional servers to manage. It runs on Supabase Edge Functions, which are small pieces of code that run close to the user (at the "edge" of the network) for fast response times.

**What this means:**
- The system scales automatically â€” no need to provision servers
- Cold starts are fast (~500ms) because the code is lightweight
- We pay only for what we use â€” cost-efficient at low scale, scalable at high scale
- 105+ edge functions handle everything from AI generation to memory consolidation to wearable sync

The database is PostgreSQL (a professional-grade relational database) hosted on Supabase. It has:
- 200+ tables organized by domain (consciousness, sessions, memory, loops, community, enterprise, etc.)
- Row-level security (users can only see their own data)
- Vector extensions for memory embeddings (semantic search across user memories)









## The Frontend










The frontend is a React application â€” a modern, responsive web app that works on desktop, mobile, and as a progressive web app (installable, offline-capable).

**Key capabilities:**
- **Responsive:** Works on phone, tablet, and desktop
- **Offline-capable:** Core functions work without internet
- **Installable:** Can be added to home screen like a native app
- **Mobile-native wrapper:** Available as iOS and Android app via Capacitor
- **60+ languages:** Fully internationalized

The app uses XState (state machines) to manage complex flows like sessions, journals, and onboarding. This ensures the app behaves predictably and can recover from interruptions (e.g., a phone call during a session).









## The AI Layer










The AI layer integrates three providers:

- **GPT-4 / Claude:** Text generation (teaching, reflection, coaching)
- **ElevenLabs:** Text-to-speech (master voices)
- **Deepgram:** Speech-to-text (voice input)
- **OpenAI:** Embeddings (memory vector search)

The AI is not a single chatbot. It's a dynamic system that reconfigures itself every turn based on the Adaptive Engine's directive. The AI has access to:
- The user's full consciousness profile
- Their active loops and patterns
- Their life domain stressors
- Their session history
- Their memory vault
- Their current state









## The Content Pipeline










Content is managed through a combination of:
- **Static content:** Hard-coded in the app (loops, programs, practices, wisdom cards)
- **Dynamic content:** AI-generated per session (teaching text, reflection prompts)
- **User-generated content:** Journal entries, practice ratings, reflections
- **Community content:** Shared observations, reactions

The static content is the foundation â€” it's what makes the system feel curated and authoritative. The dynamic content is what makes it feel personal and responsive.
