## How We Make Money







## Consumer (B2C)

**Subscription Model:**
- Freemium: 7-day showroom programs (no signup)
- Free tier: Limited access (5 practices, basic journaling)
- Premium: $10-15/month for full access
- Credits: Pay-per-use for AI-generated sessions (paise-denominated, Razorpay)

**Unit Economics:**
- CAC (Customer Acquisition Cost): <$20 (content marketing, SEO, community)
- LTV (Lifetime Value): >$100 (2+ year average subscription)
- LTV:CAC ratio: >3:1
- Gross margin: >85% (digital product, no COGS)

**Market:**
- Global meditation market: $10B â†’ $20B (2025)
- Addressable market (consciousness + AI + wellness): ~$100B







## Enterprise (B2B)

**Licensing Model:**

- Per-seat pricing: $5-10/month per employee
- Custom programs: $25K-100K per org program design
- Analytics & reporting: $5K-20K/month

**Target Customers:**
- Tech companies (high stress, high mental health awareness)
- Healthcare systems (burnout prevention, provider wellness)
- Financial services (high performance, high stress)
- Educational institutions (student mental health)
- Government agencies (first responder wellness)

**Market:**

- Addressable (consciousness + AI layer): ~$10B







## Research Licensing (Long-Term)

**Model:**

- Publish validation studies and build academic credibility

**Market:** Niche but high-margin, builds long-term IP defensibility







## Competitive Advantages







## Why We're Uniquely Positioned










**1. We Have a Model, Not Just a Product**

Every competitor has a product â€” a set of practices, a chatbot, a wearable integration. We have a model â€” a mathematically-defined, empirically-testable theory of human development that powers every decision.

This means:
- Every feature is coherent (not a collection of random tools)
- Every personalization decision has a reason
- Every progression path is defined
- Every outcome is measurable

**2. We're Designed to Be Unnecessary**

Every competitor optimizes for engagement. We optimize for liberation. This is not just a philosophical difference â€” it's a business model difference:

- Lower churn (users don't feel guilty about leaving)
- Higher LTV (users stay longer because they're actually growing)
- Better word-of-mouth (users recommend because it works, not because they're addicted)
- Stronger brand ("anti-app" is a moat)

**3. We Have Depth of Content**

270+ interactive games. 120+ programs. 300+ behavioral patterns. 25+ authentic practices. No competitor comes close. This depth:
- Makes the experience feel rich and varied
- Allows for hyper-personalization
- Creates switching costs (users invest time in exploring the library)
- Enables long-term engagement without repetition

**4. We're Multi-Modal**

We don't just read what the user types. We read:
- Their wearables (HRV, sleep, activity)
- Their calendar (meeting load, time of day)
- Their email (sentiment, tone)
- Their chat (Slack, Teams)
- Their development tools (GitHub, Linear)

This creates a richer, more accurate model of the user's state than any text-only or wearable-only competitor.

**5. We Have Enterprise Readiness**

Most wellness startups ignore B2B. We've built a complete enterprise module â€” org management, wellness challenges, quarterly reports, people intelligence, manager coaching. Enterprise is the fastest path to revenue and the most defensible market.









## Market Timing







## Why Now?

Three trends make this the right moment:

**1. AI Is Finally Capable of Nuanced, State-Aware Interaction**

GPT-4 and Claude can maintain context, adapt tone, and hold space. The prerequisites for a true consciousness companion are finally available. Five years ago, this would have been impossible.

**2. Wearables Have Mainstreamed**

Apple Watch, Oura, Whoop â€” people now have continuous physiological data. The infrastructure for multi-modal signal fusion exists. We're not asking users to buy new devices; we're integrating with what they already have.

**3. The Mental Health Crisis Is Forcing Innovation**

Post-pandemic, traditional therapy is oversubscribed. Digital therapeutics are getting FDA approval. The market is ready for something beyond meditation apps. The time is right for a new category.

# THE STRATEGIC NARRATIVE







## What Is This Company?

ConsciousOS is building the **operating system for human consciousness development** â€” the way Windows is the operating system for computers, ConsciousOS is the operating system for how you think, feel, and show up in the world.

We don't sell meditation. We don't sell coaching. We sell a system that **knows you better than you know yourself** and gives you exactly what you need, when you need it â€” then intentionally steps back as you grow.

This is a category-defining company. There is no "ConsciousOS competitor" because there is no category called "consciousness operating systems" yet. We're creating it.









## The Problem We Solve







## The Engagement Trap

Every wellness app on the market is designed for the same thing: **keep the user engaged**. More sessions. More streaks. More notifications. More time in the app.

This is the wrong goal.

The people who most need these tools â€” people struggling with anxiety, depression, trauma, meaninglessness â€” are often the people who feel most guilty about not using them. The app that demands daily engagement becomes another source of shame. The 10-day streak broken becomes evidence of failure.

**The engagement model is creating the problem it claims to solve.**









## The Consciousness Gap

Traditional therapy is expensive ($150-300/session), time-bound (50 minutes), and human-dependent (therapist availability, fit, scheduling).

Meditation apps are cheap ($10-15/month), always available, but **generic** â€” they give the same practice to everyone regardless of where they are in their development.

Coaching programs are structured but static â€” a 30-day program is the same for every participant.

**What's missing is a system that actually adapts to you â€” your state, your patterns, your development level â€” and evolves as you evolve.**









## The Solution: A Consciousness Operating System

ConsciousOS solves this through four proprietary systems:

### 1. A Consciousness Model That Actually Maps Development

We've built the most granular consciousness-development model ever created:
- **11 levels** of consciousness development (from "I am my thoughts" to "only one exists")
- **96 dimensions** of awareness (thought, emotion, body, action, connection, spirit)
- **300+ behavioral patterns** (loops) with root-cause analysis
- **6 life domains** (relationships, work, health, etc.)
- **8 hidden psychological stages** that drive how we interact with users

No other product has this depth of modeling. Headspace has 10 practices. We have a map of the entire territory.

### 2. An AI That Adapts in Real-Time

Every time a user interacts with ConsciousOS, our engine:
- Reads their current state (energy, emotion, awareness, resistance)
- Detects hidden patterns (loops, samskaras, edge cases)
- Selects the right "cognitive lens" (2-3 AI personas per turn)
- Adjusts the AI's behavior (insight depth, language, response length)
- Injects a **live system prompt** that reconfigures the AI for this specific moment

This isn't a static chatbot. It's a dynamic system that meets you where you are.

### 3. A Content Engine That Scales Personalization

We have:
- **270+ interactive practice experiences** (not videos â€” interactive exercises)
- **120+ structured programs** across awareness, emotion, body, relationships, purpose
- **25+ authentic practices** from world traditions (Ramana, Krishna, Patanjali, Buddha, etc.)
- **50+ wisdom cards** with neuroscience commentary
- **8-band stage handbook** mapping each level to specific practices, traps, and pathways

The system selects from this library based on your current state, your weakest dimensions, your active loops, and your life domain stressors. Two users at the same level get different practices. The same user gets different practices on different days.


## What We've Built (Non-Technical)

Every other app optimizes for retention. We optimize for **liberation**.









## The Business Model







## Consumer (B2C)

**Revenue Streams:**
- **Freemium:** 7-day showroom programs (no signup required) â†’ conversion funnel
- **Subscription:** $10-15/month for full access
- **Credits:** Pay-per-use for AI-generated sessions (paise-denominated, Razorpay)
- **Programs:** Premium structured programs (lifetime access pricing)

**Unit Economics (Target):**
- CAC: <$20 (content marketing, SEO, community)
- LTV: >$100 (2+ year average subscription)
- LTV:CAC: >3:1
- Gross margin: >85% (digital product, no COGS)

**Market Size:**
- Global meditation market: $10B â†’ $20B (2025)
- Global wellness market: $4.5T
- Addressable market (consciousness + AI + wellness): ~$100B

### Enterprise (B2B) â€” Higher Probability Path

**Revenue Streams:**
- **Platform licensing:** $10K-50K/month per organization
- **Per-seat pricing:** $5-10/month per employee
- **Custom programs:** $25K-100K per org program design
- **Analytics & reporting:** $5K-20K/month for org consciousness dashboards

**Target Customers:**

- Healthcare systems (burnout prevention, provider wellness)

- Government agencies (first responder wellness)

- Addressable (consciousness + AI layer): ~$10B

### Research Licensing (Long-Term)

**Market Size:** Niche but high-margin, builds long-term IP defensibility









## The Product







## What You Experience

**Morning:**
You open the app. The screen shows your "morning gate" â€” a soft, time-appropriate greeting from your guide. It knows you had a rough night (wearable data). It suggests a 10-minute breath practice, not the 30-minute deep dive you had scheduled. It says, "Gentle return." You practice. The orb animates in sync with your breath. You feel seen, not managed.

**Midday:**
A subtle notification. Your calendar was back-to-back meetings. Your Slack tone was sharp. The app suggests a 60-second "soften jaw" micro-practice. You tap it. Done. The AI doesn't ask you to journal or reflect â€” it knows you're in meeting mode.

**Evening:**
A longer session. Today's theme is "Surrender in Action." Your guide (Ramana, or Krishna, or a modern voice) introduces the theme. You do a 15-minute practice targeting your weakest dimension (clarity). You journal for 5 minutes. The AI asks one question: "What arose for you?" You type a sentence. The AI doesn't give advice. It reflects back: "You noticed the difference between effort and allowing." You feel understood.

**Night:**
Before bed, the app shows your "night sanctuary." A 5-minute body scan. A dream journal prompt. The AI presence is almost gone â€” just a soft presence. You practiced today. You don't need more. The app knows this. It says, "Rest now. We'll continue tomorrow."

**Over time:**
Weeks pass. The practices get shorter but deeper. The app starts suggesting silence instead of sessions. It says, "Today, just sit with this." You realize you're thinking about the practice even when the app is closed. The app has become part of your awareness, not a separate tool you need.









## What We've Built (Non-Technical)

**Content & Curriculum:**
- 270+ interactive practice experiences (the largest library of its kind)
- 120+ structured programs (from beginner to advanced)
- 300+ behavioral pattern definitions with root-cause analysis
- 25+ practices from world traditions (authenticated, not adapted)
- 50+ wisdom cards with scientific commentary
- 8-band stage handbook (what to practice, what to avoid, how to progress)

**Technology (What It Does, Not How):**
- A system that knows your consciousness state and adapts in real-time
- An AI that changes its behavior based on your psychological state
- A recommendation engine that selects the right practice from 270+ options
- A memory system that remembers your breakthroughs and patterns
- A multi-modal signal system that reads your wearables, calendar, and communication patterns

**Platform:**
- Mobile app (iOS + Android via Capacitor)
- Progressive web app (installable, offline-capable)
- Desktop web app
- 60+ languages (including Hinglish for Indian market)
- Enterprise dashboard for organizations
- Admin panel for operations









## The Market Opportunity







## The Wellness Market Is Massive and Fragmented

**Global Wellness Market:** $4.5T (2022) â†’ projected $7T (2025)  
**Meditation/Mindfulness:** $10B â†’ $20B (2025)  
**Mental Health Apps:** $5B â†’ $12B (2027)  
**AI Companions:** $1.5B â†’ $4B (2028)  
**Enterprise Wellness:** $60B  

These markets are converging. The next wave of wellness products won't be "meditation apps" or "AI companions" â€” they'll be **consciousness platforms** that integrate all of these capabilities.

We're building that platform.









## Why Now?

Three trends make this the right time:

1. **AI is finally capable of nuanced, state-aware interaction.** GPT-4 and Claude can maintain context, adapt tone, and hold space â€” the prerequisites for a true consciousness companion.

2. **Wearables have mainstreamed.** Apple Watch, Oura, Whoop â€” people now have continuous physiological data. We can read HRV, sleep, activity, and use it to inform practice selection.

3. **The mental health crisis is forcing innovation.** Post-pandemic, traditional therapy is oversubscribed. Digital therapeutics are getting FDA approval. The market is ready for something beyond meditation apps.









## Competitive Landscape







## Who We Compete With (And Why We're Different)

| Competitor | What They Do | Why We're Different |
|------------|--------------|---------------------|
| **Headspace** | Meditation videos and audio | Generic content, no personalization, no consciousness model |
| **Calm** | Sleep + meditation stories | No AI, no state adaptation, engagement-optimized |
| **Waking Up** (Sam Harris) | Philosophy + meditation | No engine, no personalization, single-teacher model |
| **Woebot** | AI therapy chatbot | Clinical focus, no spiritual/practice dimension |
| **Replika** | AI companion | No wellness focus, no practice engine, no progression model |
| **Noom** | Behavioral change | Psychology-based, no AI, no consciousness model |
| **Whoop/Oura** | Wearables + insights | Data collection, no action layer, no consciousness framework |

**The key differentiator:** We have a **model of human development** (the 11-band consciousness model) that no competitor has. Everything else â€” the content, the AI, the signal fusion â€” flows from this model.

Without the model, we're just another wellness app. With the model, we're a category of one.









## The Team & Execution







## Current State

The company is founder-led with a strong engineering team. We've built:
- A complete product (feature-rich, production-ready pending hardening)
- Massive content library (270+ games, 120+ programs, 300+ patterns)
- Enterprise-ready architecture (B2B module, org management, analytics)
- Global readiness (60+ locales, multi-modal AI, Razorpay for India)

## What We Need

To reach the next level, we need:

1. **4-6 weeks of production hardening** (security, testing, architectural cleanup)
2. **A validation study** (empirical proof the consciousness model works)
3. **Enterprise sales leadership** (to capture the B2B opportunity)
4. **Research partnerships** (academic credibility)
5. **Marketing/brand leadership** (to communicate the "anti-app" thesis)

None of these are technical. They're business execution.

**Exit (Year 3-4):** $20M-$100M strategic acquisition or Series A at $50M-$100M valuation

## Base Scenario (Path B: B2B Engine)

**Year 1:**
- Revenue: $200K-$500K (enterprise pilots)
- Users: 10,000-25,000 (B2C)
- Burn: $1.5M-$2M
- Milestones: 3-5 enterprise customers, product-market fit in B2B

**Year 2:**
- Revenue: $1.5M-$3M (enterprise + B2C subscriptions)
- Users: 50,000-100,000
- Burn: $3M-$5M
- Milestones: 10+ enterprise customers, D30 retention >25%

**Year 3:**
- Revenue: $5M-$10M
- Users: 200,000-500,000
- Burn: $5M-$8M
- Milestones: 25+ enterprise customers, path to profitability

**Exit (Year 4-5):** $100M-$500M strategic acquisition or Series B at $100M-$200M valuation

## Bull Case (Path A: Consumer + B2B)

**Year 1:**
- Revenue: $500K-$1M (mixed)
- Users: 25,000-50,000
- Burn: $2M-$3M
- Milestones: Validation published, enterprise pilot, strong D30 retention

**Year 2:**
- Revenue: $3M-$8M
- Users: 100,000-200,000
- Burn: $4M-$6M
- Milestones: 10+ enterprise customers, NPS >50

**Year 3:**
- Revenue: $15M-$30M
- Users: 500,000-1M
- Burn: $8M-$15M
- Milestones: Path to profitability, category leadership

**Exit (Year 5-7):** $500M-$1B IPO or strategic acquisition

## The Bottom Line

We have built a company with:
- **Real IP** (consciousness model, adaptive AI)
- **Real product** (feature-complete, ready for market)
- **Real content** (270+ games, 120+ programs, 300+ patterns)
- **Real market** ($100B+ addressable, converging trends)
- **Real path to value** (B2C, B2B, or research licensing)

The question isn't whether this can be a billion-dollar company. The question is **which path we take and how fast we execute**.

The IP supports a billion-dollar outcome. The execution requires 12-18 months of focused business development, validation, and enterprise traction.









# ROADMAP: NEXT 12 MONTHS







## Phase 1: Production Readiness (Months 1-2)

**Goal:** Make the product stable, secure, and demonstrable

**Deliverables:**
- Security hardening (remove prompt injection, add rate limiting, server-side credit checks)
- Testing foundation (core engines tested, CI/CD pipeline)
- Architectural cleanup (consolidate session systems, reduce technical debt)
- Performance optimization (bundle size, load times)
- Monitoring & observability (error tracking, health checks)

**Investment:** $200K-$400K (2 engineers, 2 months)

**Milestone:** Production-ready product that can be demoed to enterprise prospects







## Phase 2: Validation (Months 3-6)

**Goal:** Prove the consciousness model works

**Deliverables:**
- Longitudinal study design (partner with research institution)
- Baseline data collection (existing users)
- Correlation analysis (V-Level vs established psychometrics)
- First validation paper (target: Consciousness and Cognition journal)
- Public-facing validation dashboard

**Investment:** $300K-$600K (research partner, data analysis, paper preparation)

**Milestone:** Published validation study providing empirical evidence for the model







## Phase 3: Enterprise Launch (Months 7-9)

**Goal:** First enterprise customers and revenue

**Deliverables:**
- Enterprise sales deck and demo environment
- Pilot program with 3-5 customers (tech, healthcare, finance)
- Enterprise onboarding workflow
- Case study development
- Pricing and packaging finalized

**Investment:** $150K-$300K (sales lead, demo environment, customer success)

**Milestone:** $500K+ in annualized enterprise contracts







## Phase 4: Scale (Months 10-12)

**Goal:** Prove the model at scale

**Deliverables:**
- 10,000+ active users
- 10+ enterprise customers
- $1M+ ARR
- Published validation study
- Series A pitch deck

**Investment:** $500K-$1M (marketing, sales, content, engineering)

**Milestone:** Fundable company with traction, validation, and revenue







# KEY DECISIONS REQUIRED







## Decision 1: Consumer vs B2B Focus

**Option A: B2C First**
- Pros: Larger market, brand control, direct user relationship
- Cons: Higher CAC, longer path to revenue, more competition
- Timeline to revenue: 12-18 months

**Option B: B2B First**
- Pros: Faster revenue, higher LTV, less competition, validation through orgs
- Cons: Smaller market, longer sales cycles, product constraints
- Timeline to revenue: 6-9 months

**Option C: Parallel**
- Pros: Diversified revenue, fastest path to scale
- Cons: Requires more capital, more complex execution
- Timeline to revenue: 9-12 months

**Recommendation:** Option C â€” maintain B2C brand and product while pursuing enterprise pilots in parallel. B2B revenue funds B2C growth.









## Decision 2: Spiritual vs Secular Framing

**Option A: Spiritual Framing (Current)**
- Pros: Authentic, differentiated, defensible IP
- Cons: Limits mainstream appeal, requires market education

**Option B: Secular Framing**
- Pros: Mainstream appeal, enterprise-friendly, broader market
- Cons: Loses authenticity, harder to differentiate from Headspace

**Option C: Dual Framing**
- Pros: Best of both â€” spiritual for B2C, secular for B2B
- Cons: Requires maintaining two brand narratives

**Recommendation:** Option C â€” build secular language layer for enterprise while keeping spiritual framing for consumer. The model is the same; the language changes.









## Decision 3: Open Source vs Closed

**Option A: Closed (Current)**
- Pros: Maximum IP protection, licensing potential
- Cons: Slower validation, less community contribution

**Option B: Open Source Core**
- Pros: Faster validation, community trust, academic credibility
- Cons: IP exposure, harder to monetize

**Option C: Research Partnership**
- Pros: Academic credibility without full open source
- Cons: Slower, requires partnership management

**Recommendation:** Option C â€” partner with 1-2 research institutions for validation studies. Keep core IP closed but publish validation data transparently.









## Decision 4: Geography

**Option A: Global (Current)**
- Pros: Largest market, first-mover advantage
- Cons: Complex localization, regulatory fragmentation

**Option B: US First**
- Pros: Largest wellness market, simplest regulatory environment
- Cons: More competition, higher CAC

**Option C: India First**
- Pros: Lower CAC, Razorpay integration, spiritual market familiarity
- Cons: Smaller market, lower ARPU

**Recommendation:** Option B (US first) with India as secondary market. The US enterprise wellness market is the fastest path to revenue. India is the fastest path to user growth.

---







# APPENDIX: THE INVESTMENT CASE







## What We're Asking For

**Series A Target:** $3M-$5M  
**Pre-Money Valuation:** $15M-$25M (pre-validation)  
**Post-Money Valuation:** $18M-$30M  

**Use of Funds:**
- 40% Product & Engineering (hardening, features, scaling)
- 25% Enterprise Sales (hiring, demos, pilots)
- 20% Research & Validation (studies, academic partnerships)
- 15% Marketing & Brand (content, community, SEO)

**Milestones for Next Round (18 months):**
- 50,000+ active users
- 10+ enterprise customers
- $2M+ ARR
- Published validation study
- D30 retention >25%

**Exit Scenarios:**
- 3 years: $100M-$500M (strategic acquisition by wellness platform or HR tech)
- 5 years: $500M-$1B (category leader, IPO candidate)
- 7 years: $1B+ (category-defining platform)

## Why Invest

1. **Category-defining IP:** The consciousness model is 3-5 years ahead of any competitor
2. **Massive market:** $100B+ addressable, converging trends (AI + wellness + enterprise)
3. **Counter-intuitive positioning:** "Anti-app" is a brand moat, not a feature
4. **Proven team:** Built a complex, production-ready system with limited resources
5. **Multiple paths to value:** B2C, B2B, research licensing, strategic acquisition
6. **Timing:** AI + wearables + mental health crisis = perfect storm

## Risks & Mitigations

| Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |
|------|-----------|--------|-----------|
| Validation fails | Medium | High | Parallel research tracks, secular fallback |
| Enterprise sales slow | Medium | High | B2C revenue as backup, freemium conversion |
| Competition enters | Low | Medium | 3-5 year head start, IP moat |
| LLM costs spike | Low | Medium | Model routing, caching, cost monitoring |
| Team scaling | Medium | Medium | Strong hiring pipeline, advisor network |

---







# CONCLUSION







## The One-Sentence Pitch

**ConsciousOS is building the operating system for human consciousness development â€” an AI-powered platform that adapts in real-time to your psychological state, gives you exactly what you need, and intentionally fades as you grow.**

## The Three-Minute Pitch

1. **The Problem:** Every wellness app optimizes for engagement, creating dependency and guilt. Therapy is expensive and inaccessible. There's no system that actually adapts to your development level.

2. **The Solution:** We've built a proprietary consciousness model (11 levels, 96 dimensions, 300+ patterns) and an AI engine that adapts in real-time. We have 270+ interactive practices, 120+ programs, and a design philosophy that makes the app part of your awareness rather than a separate tool.

3. **The Business:** Consumer subscriptions ($10-15/month) + enterprise licensing ($10K-50K/month) + research partnerships. $100B+ addressable market. Path to $100M-$1B exit in 3-7 years.

## The Ask

We're raising $3M-$5M Series A to:
1. Harden the product (2 months)
2. Validate the model (3-4 months)
3. Launch enterprise (6-9 months)
4. Scale to 50K users and $2M ARR (12 months)

In exchange, we're offering 15-20% equity at a $15M-$25M pre-money valuation.

**The IP supports a billion-dollar outcome. The next 12 months will prove whether we can execute on it.**

===== BEGIN: SESSION_FLOW.md (D:\final\sat-yug\SESSION_FLOW.md) =====

# Session Flow Orchestrator

## Overview

The Session Flow Orchestrator provides structured AI prompts for **Morning**, **Evening**, and **Night** sessions. It ensures the AI interacts with users in a personalized, theme-centered way that adapts to their journey state.

## Architecture

### Files Created

1. **`src/lib/sessionFlow.ts`** â€” Core prompt generation logic
2. **`src/hooks/useSessionFlow.ts`** â€” React hook for managing session state
3. **`src/pages/Guide.tsx`** â€” Updated to use structured prompts

## How It Works

### 1. **Morning Session Flow** (5AM - 12PM)

**Phases:**
1. **Greeting** â€” Warm welcome using their name, streak, journey day
2. **Theme Introduction** â€” "Today's theme is {title}"
3. **Why This Theme** â€” Connects theme_rationale to their band level, focus dimension, patterns
4. **How This Helps** â€” 2-3 specific benefits tied to their real life
5. **Opening Question** â€” "How did you sleep? Any dreams?"
6. **Practice** â€” After 2-3 exchanges, suggests practice based on their state
7. **Integration** â€” "What's one thing you're carrying forward?"

**Special Scenarios:**
- **Returning after 3+ days**: No guilt, 5 min only, "path celebrates return"
- **Long streak (7+)**: Challenge deeper, extend practice time
- **Normal**: Full structured flow

### 2. **Evening Session Flow** (5PM - 9PM)

**Phases:**
1. **Greeting + Day Review** â€” "Good evening. The day is settling."
2. **Reflection on Specifics** â€” References morning session, tasks, practice
3. **Celebrate Wins** â€” Even ONE moment of awareness is huge
4. **Explore Struggles** â€” Without guilt, extract learning
5. **Gratitude Practice** â€” 3 things: grateful for, made smile, appreciate
6. **Lessons Extraction** â€” "What did today teach you?"
7. **Connect to Tomorrow** â€” Preview next theme, get one commitment
8. **Closing** â€” Review: theme, clarity moment, lesson, gratitude

### 3. **Night Sanctuary Flow** (9PM - 5AM)

**Phases:**
1. **Greeting + Wind Down** â€” Gentle, releasing tone
2. **Quick Check-in** â€” "How's your body? Tired? Restless?"
3. **Practice Based on State**:
   - Restless â†’ 15 min Yoga Nidra
   - Peaceful â†’ 5 min gratitude
   - Heavy â†’ 10 min gentle breathwork
4. **Plant One Seed** â€” Theme affirmation for sleep
5. **Closing** â€” "Sleep well. The path holds you."

**Rules:**
- NO deep inquiry
- NO analysis
- Keep everything short, gentle
- Redirect to rest if they start analyzing

## Context Data Used

The system gathers:

```typescript
{
  user: {
    userName, streak, journeyDay, bandLevel, bandName, 
    focusDimension, language
  },
  theme: {
    title, description, theme_rationale, learning_focus,
    daily_intentions, action_items, affirmation, focus_areas
  },
  morningSession: { completed, summary, insights },
  dayActivity: {
    journalEntries, practiceDone, tasksCompleted, 
    tasksMissed, dominantMood, energyLevel
  },
  missedDays, yesterdaySummary
}
```

## Usage in Guide Page

```typescript
// Hook automatically builds system prompt + first message
const sessionFlow = useSessionFlow({
  sessionType: slot,
  userName,
  streak,
  journeyDay,
  theme: themeData?.theme,
  // ... other context
});

// First message is injected when session starts
useEffect(() => {
  if (isActive && sessionId && messageCount === 0) {
    send({ type: "STREAM_DELTA", content: sessionFlow.firstMessage });
  }
}, [isActive, sessionId]);

// System prompt is passed to AI for all subsequent messages
await streamChat({
  aiContext: sessionFlow.systemPrompt,
  // ...
});
```

## Customization

### To modify morning flow:
Edit `buildMorningSpecificPrompt()` in `sessionFlow.ts`

### To modify evening flow:
Edit `buildEveningSpecificPrompt()` in `sessionFlow.ts`

### To change first message templates:
Edit `buildMorningFirstMessage()`, `buildEveningFirstMessage()`, or `buildNightFirstMessage()`

### To add new phases:
1. Add to `SessionPhase` type
2. Update `advancePhase()` logic in hook
3. Add phase-specific instructions in system prompt

## TODO

- [ ] Fetch morning session data from today's sessions
- [ ] Fetch day activity from journal/tasks/practice
- [ ] Track phase progression in session state
- [ ] Add midday micro check-in flow
- [ ] Add voice tone adaptation based on session type

===== BEGIN: README.md (D:\final\sat-yug\README.md) =====

# HOPE â€” A companion for becoming.

HOPE is an AI-guided companion for consciousness development. It helps you see your patterns, choose your practices, and become who you're meant to be â€” one session at a time.

## Features

- **Dynamic daily themes** that evolve with your journey
- **120+ structured programs** across self-inquiry, pranayama, chakra work, shadow integration, and more
- **AI guide chat** with 60+ master voices adapted to your spiritual depth
- **Consciousness engine** that maps your progress across 100 dimensions
- **Mirror sessions** for self-reflection and loop detection
- **Enterprise wellness** for organizations
- **Multi-modal AI** â€” voice, text, and generative sessions
- **60+ languages** supported

## Tech Stack

- **Frontend**: React 18 + TypeScript + Vite
- **Styling**: Tailwind CSS + shadcn/ui + Framer Motion
- **Backend**: Supabase (PostgreSQL, Auth, Edge Functions)
- **AI**: Groq, Google Gemini, ElevenLabs, Deepgram
- **Mobile**: Capacitor (iOS + Android)
- **State**: XState + TanStack Query

## Getting Started

```sh
git clone <repo-url>
cd <repo-name>
npm install
npm run dev
```

## Environment Variables

Copy `.env.example` to `.env` and fill in your Supabase and AI provider keys.

## Build

```sh
npm run build
npm run preview
```

## Test

```sh
npm test
```

## License

Proprietary â€” all rights reserved.

===== END: README.md =====

===== vite.config.ts =====
import { defineConfig, type Plugin } from "vite";
import react from "@vitejs/plugin-react-swc";
import { VitePWA } from "vite-plugin-pwa";
import path from "path";
import fs from "fs";

const BUILD_ID = Date.now().toString();

// Emit /version.json into the build output so the running app can poll it
// and force a hard refresh when a newer build is deployed (mobile sees stale shell).
function emitVersionJsonPlugin(): Plugin {
  return {
    name: "emit-version-json",
    closeBundle() {
      const out = path.resolve(__dirname, "dist", "version.json");
      try {
        fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(out), { recursive: true });
        fs.writeFileSync(out, JSON.stringify({ buildId: BUILD_ID }));
      } catch (_e) { /* non-fatal */ }
    },
  };
}

/**
 * Serve bundled images from the Supabase CDN as WebP.
 *
 * Images under src/assets are uploaded to the `media` bucket by
 * scripts/push-images-to-cdn.mjs, which records the mapping in
 * src/lib/media/cdn-images.json. This plugin resolves any import of a
 * mapped image to its CDN URL instead of emitting a copy into dist/assets.
 *
 * Why it matters:
 *   - Removes ~22 MB of imagery from the deploy and from the app origin.
 *   - The bucket is served with `public, max-age=31536000, immutable`
 *     (scripts/set-bucket-cache.mjs), so repeat views are edge hits.
 *   - The CDN's /render/image/ endpoint converts PNG/JPG to WebP on the
 *     fly. Across the 299 uploaded images that is 61 MB -> 23 MB (62%
 *     smaller) with no loss of displayed size, because the transform is
 *     format-only and does not resize.
 *
 * If the manifest is missing (fresh clone, upload not run yet) the plugin
 * is a no-op and images bundle locally exactly as before.
 */
function cdnImagePlugin(): Plugin {
  const manifestPath = path.resolve(__dirname, "src/lib/media/cdn-images.json");
  if (!fs.existsSync(manifestPath)) return { name: "cdn-images" };

  let manifest: { images?: Record<string, { url?: string; render_url?: string; size?: number }> } = {};
  try {
    manifest = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(manifestPath, "utf8"));
  } catch {
    return { name: "cdn-images" };
  }
  const images = manifest.images || {};
  if (Object.keys(images).length === 0) return { name: "cdn-images" };

  // Format-only WebP conversion. No width/height, so the intrinsic size is
  // unchanged and every existing layout keeps working.
  const WEBP = "?format=webp&quality=85";

  // local absolute path -> CDN url
  const byAbsPath = new Map<string, string>();
  for (const [rel, entry] of Object.entries(images)) {
    const url = entry?.render_url ? `${entry.render_url}${WEBP}` : entry?.url;
    if (!url) continue;
    byAbsPath.set(normalize(path.resolve(__dirname, "src", rel)), url);
  }

  return {
    name: "cdn-images",
    enforce: "pre",
    resolveId(source, importer) {
      if (!/\.(png|jpe?g|webp|gif|svg)(\?.*)?$/i.test(source)) return null;

      // Strip the query so `?url` style imports resolve the same way.
      const bare = source.split("?")[0];

      // By the time this plugin runs, Vite has already applied the "@/"
      // alias, so `source` is usually an absolute path. Relative specifiers
      // are still possible for imports that come from inside src/assets.
      let abs: string;
      if (path.isAbsolute(bare)) {
        abs = bare;
      } else if (source.startsWith(".")) {
        if (!importer) return null;
        abs = path.resolve(path.dirname(importer.split("?")[0]), bare);
      } else {
        return null;
      }

      const cdn = byAbsPath.get(normalize(abs));
      if (!cdn) return null;

      // A virtual module exporting the CDN URL as the default export, which
      // is exactly what Vite's own asset pipeline produces for
      // `import img from "./x.png"`.
      return `\0cdn-image:${normalize(abs)}`;
    },
    load(id) {
      if (!id.startsWith("\0cdn-image:")) return null;
      const cdn = byAbsPath.get(id.slice("\0cdn-image:".length));
      if (!cdn) return null;
      return `export default ${JSON.stringify(cdn)};`;
    },
  };
}

/** Windows-safe path key: forward slashes, lowercase drive letter. */
function normalize(p: string): string {
  return path.resolve(p).replace(/\\/g, "/").replace(/^([A-Z]):/, (m) => m.toLowerCase());
}

/**
 * Vendor splitting.
 *
 * Without this, Rollup folds every shared dependency back into the entry
 * chunk (measured before this change: 945 kB raw / 288 kB gzip in one file).
 * Splitting the stable, rarely-changing vendor libs into their own
 * long-lived chunks means a content change in app code does NOT invalidate
 * the vendor bytes, so returning visitors re-download only the changed chunk.
 *
 * Groups are chosen by "changes independently of app code", not by size:
 *   - react*  : core runtime, changes on a React upgrade only
 *   - router  : react-router-dom
 *   - query   : @tanstack/react-query (+persist)
 *   - supabase: @supabase/supabase-js
 *   - three   : three + @react-three/* (the GPU orb)
 *   - xstate  : xstate + @xstate/react
 *   - date    : date-fns
 *
 * IMPORTANT: recharts and react-markdown are deliberately NOT listed here.
 * Both are reachable only from lazy routes, and Rollup's default splitting
 * already keeps them off the critical path. Claiming them with manualChunks
 * made things WORSE: Rollup hoisted their shared helpers (clsx,
 * commonjsHelpers) into the manual chunk, which then forced the whole
 * 410 kB recharts bundle to become a static dependency of the entry.
 *
 * framer-motion and @radix-ui are also not claimed: both are used by
 * eagerly-loaded modules, so splitting them only moved the bytes around
 * without taking anything off the critical path.
 *
 * `three`, `xstate` and `date-fns` are safe to claim because their
 * dependencies do not overlap with anything on the entry's static path.
 */
function manualChunks(id: string) {
  if (!id.includes("node_modules")) return undefined;

  // Order matters: most specific first.
  if (/[\\/]node_modules[\\/](three|@react-three)[\\/]/.test(id)) return "vendor-three";
  if (/[\\/]node_modules[\\/]@tanstack[\\/]/.test(id)) return "vendor-query";
  if (/[\\/]node_modules[\\/]@supabase[\\/]/.test(id)) return "vendor-supabase";
  if (/[\\/]node_modules[\\/](xstate|@xstate)[\\/]/.test(id)) return "vendor-xstate";
  if (/[\\/]node_modules[\\/]date-fns[\\/]/.test(id)) return "vendor-date";
  if (/[\\/]node_modules[\\/](react|react-dom|scheduler|react-is|use-sync-external-store)[\\/]/.test(id)) return "vendor-react";
  if (/[\\/]node_modules[\\/]react-router(-dom)?[\\/]/.test(id)) return "vendor-router";

  return undefined;
}

// https://vitejs.dev/config/
export default defineConfig(({ mode }) => {
  const isProd = mode === "production";
  // Escape hatch used by scripts/measure-bundle.mjs to A/B the effect of
  // manualChunks against Rollup's default splitting.
  const skipManualChunks = process.env.SKIP_MANUAL_CHUNKS === "1";

  return {
    server: {
      host: "::",
      port: 8080,
      hmr: {
        overlay: false,
      },
    },
    plugins: [
      react(),
      cdnImagePlugin(),
      emitVersionJsonPlugin(),
      VitePWA({
        // The app already polls /version.json to detect a newer build, so
        // the service worker must not serve a stale shell on its own.
        // "autoUpdate" installs a new worker as soon as one is available and
        // the old one stops controlling the page immediately.
        registerType: "autoUpdate",
        injectRegister: "auto",
        // The manifest is hand-written at public/manifest.webmanifest (it
        // already exists and is referenced by the app shell), so the plugin
        // must not generate a competing one.
        manifest: false,
        workbox: {
          // Precache the app SHELL only, not every lazy route.
          //
          // The app has ~600 lazy route chunks totalling ~11 MB. Precaching
          // all of them would make the service worker install download 11 MB
          // on the first visit, which is worse than the problem it solves.
          // Instead we precache the shell (HTML, CSS, entry JS and the
          // vendor chunks it needs) and let route chunks be fetched and
          // cached on demand by the runtime rules below.
          globPatterns: ["index.html", "manifest.webmanifest", "favicon.ico", "favicon.png"],
          globIgnores: ["**/sw.js", "**/workbox-*.js", "**/registerSW.js"],
          // Every build output filename is content-hashed, so a changed
          // asset is a new file and the old one can be dropped.
          cleanupOutdatedCaches: true,
          clientsClaim: true,
          skipWaiting: true,
          navigateFallback: "/index.html",
          maximumFileSizeToCacheInBytes: 4 * 1024 * 1024,
          runtimeCaching: [
            {
              // Hashed JS/CSS: cache-first is safe because a changed file
              // gets a new name, so the URL is a cache key that never goes
              // stale.
              urlPattern: /\/assets\/.*\.(?:js|css)$/i,
              handler: "StaleWhileRevalidate",
              options: {
                cacheName: "app-assets",
                expiration: { maxEntries: 120, maxAgeSeconds: 60 * 60 * 24 * 30 },
                cacheableResponse: { statuses: [0, 200] },
              },
            },
            {
              // Supabase media is immutable and served with a one-year
              // max-age, so the browser already keeps it. Caching it in the
              // service worker too makes repeat visits work offline.
              urlPattern: /^https:\/\/.*\.supabase\.co\/storage\/v1\/(object|render)\/.*/i,
              handler: "CacheFirst",
              options: {
                cacheName: "supabase-media",
                expiration: { maxEntries: 300, maxAgeSeconds: 60 * 60 * 24 * 30 },
                cacheableResponse: { statuses: [0, 200] },
                rangeRequests: true,
              },
            },
            {
              urlPattern: /^https:\/\/fonts\.(?:googleapis|gstatic)\.com\/.*/i,
              handler: "StaleWhileRevalidate",
              options: {
                cacheName: "google-fonts",
                expiration: { maxEntries: 30, maxAgeSeconds: 60 * 60 * 24 * 365 },
                cacheableResponse: { statuses: [0, 200] },
              },
            },
          ],
        },
        devOptions: {
          enabled: false,
        },
      }),
    ].filter(Boolean),
    define: {
      __BUILD_ID__: JSON.stringify(BUILD_ID),
    },
    resolve: {
      alias: {
        "@": path.resolve(__dirname, "./src"),
      },
    },
    // Pre-bundle radix + heavy deps to prevent the cold-load 502 race
    // where Vite re-optimizes deps mid-HMR and serves a broken chunk.
    optimizeDeps: {
      include: [
        "@radix-ui/react-dialog",
        "@radix-ui/react-dropdown-menu",
        "@radix-ui/react-popover",
        "@radix-ui/react-tooltip",
        "@radix-ui/react-tabs",
        "@radix-ui/react-select",
        "@radix-ui/react-toast",
        "@radix-ui/react-accordion",
        "@radix-ui/react-slot",
        "react",
        "react-dom",
        "react-router-dom",
        // @react-three/fiber's reconciler is CommonJS. Served raw, the browser
        // cannot read the named exports out of it, so the GPU orb never
        // mounts in dev. Pre-bundling it fixes the interop.
        "react-reconciler",
        "react-reconciler/constants",
      ],
      // three is huge; pre-bundling it in dev avoids a multi-second stall
      // the first time the orb route is opened. @react-three/fiber has to be
      // pre-bundled too, or its CJS reconciler is served raw.
      exclude: ["@react-three/drei"],
    },
    build: {
      // Real target, not the default "modules" (= esnext-ish). es2020 keeps
      // optional chaining / nullish coalescing native while staying safe for
      // every browser Capacitor's WebView runs on.
      target: "es2020",
      cssCodeSplit: true,
      // Inline assets under 4 kB instead of emitting a file + a request.
      assetsInlineLimit: 4096,
      // Measuring gzip for 600+ chunks dominates build time; sizes are
      // tracked separately by scripts/measure-bundle.mjs.
      reportCompressedSize: false,
      // We split vendors deliberately, so a large chunk is a signal to
      // investigate, not noise to silence.
      chunkSizeWarningLimit: 400,
      minify: "esbuild",
      sourcemap: false,
      rollupOptions: {
        output: {
          manualChunks: skipManualChunks ? undefined : manualChunks,
          // Deterministic, cache-friendly names.
          chunkFileNames: "assets/[name]-[hash].js",
          entryFileNames: "assets/[name]-[hash].js",
          assetFileNames: "assets/[name]-[hash][extname]",
        },
      },
      esbuild: {
        drop: isProd ? ["debugger"] : [],
        legalComments: "none",
        target: "es2020",
      },
    },
  };
});

import type { Config } from "tailwindcss";

export default {
  darkMode: ["class"],
  content: ["./pages/**/*.{ts,tsx}", "./components/**/*.{ts,tsx}", "./app/**/*.{ts,tsx}", "./src/**/*.{ts,tsx}"],
  prefix: "",
  theme: {
    container: {
      center: true,
      padding: "2rem",
      screens: {
        "2xl": "1400px",
      },
    },
    extend: {
      fontFamily: {
        display: ["Inter", "ui-sans-serif", "system-ui", "-apple-system", "Segoe UI", "sans-serif"],
        body: ["Inter", "ui-sans-serif", "system-ui", "-apple-system", "Segoe UI", "sans-serif"],
        sans: ["Inter", "ui-sans-serif", "system-ui", "-apple-system", "Segoe UI", "sans-serif"],
        sacred: ["Cinzel", "Cormorant Garamond", "serif"],
      },
      letterSpacing: {
        tightest: "-0.045em",
        tighter: "-0.035em",
        tight: "-0.022em",
        snug: "-0.011em",
      },
      fontSize: {
        // HeyGen-scale display sizes with tight leading/tracking baked in
        "display-2xl": ["clamp(3rem, 8vw, 6.25rem)", { lineHeight: "1.02", letterSpacing: "-0.04em", fontWeight: "700" }],
        "display-xl":  ["clamp(2.5rem, 6.5vw, 5rem)",  { lineHeight: "1.04", letterSpacing: "-0.035em", fontWeight: "700" }],
        "display-lg":  ["clamp(2rem, 5vw, 3.75rem)",    { lineHeight: "1.06", letterSpacing: "-0.03em", fontWeight: "700" }],
        "display-md":  ["clamp(1.75rem, 3.6vw, 2.75rem)",{ lineHeight: "1.1",  letterSpacing: "-0.025em", fontWeight: "600" }],
        "display-sm":  ["1.5rem",   { lineHeight: "1.15", letterSpacing: "-0.02em", fontWeight: "600" }],
        "eyebrow":     ["0.75rem",  { lineHeight: "1",    letterSpacing: "0.14em", fontWeight: "600" }],
      },
      spacing: {
        // HeyGen-style generous section rhythm
        "section-sm": "4rem",
        "section":    "6rem",
        "section-lg": "9rem",
        "section-xl": "12rem",
        "gutter":     "1.5rem",
        "gutter-lg":  "2rem",
      },
      maxWidth: {
        "prose-tight": "38rem",
        "prose-wide":  "48rem",
        "content":     "72rem",
        "content-lg":  "80rem",
      },
      colors: {
        border: "hsl(var(--border))",
        input: "hsl(var(--input))",
        ring: "hsl(var(--ring))",
        background: "hsl(var(--background))",
        foreground: "hsl(var(--foreground))",
        primary: {
          DEFAULT: "hsl(var(--primary))",
          foreground: "hsl(var(--primary-foreground))",
        },
        secondary: {
          DEFAULT: "hsl(var(--secondary))",
          foreground: "hsl(var(--secondary-foreground))",
        },
        destructive: {
          DEFAULT: "hsl(var(--destructive))",
          foreground: "hsl(var(--destructive-foreground))",
        },
        muted: {
          DEFAULT: "hsl(var(--muted))",
          foreground: "hsl(var(--muted-foreground))",
        },
        accent: {
          DEFAULT: "hsl(var(--accent))",
          foreground: "hsl(var(--accent-foreground))",
        },
        popover: {
          DEFAULT: "hsl(var(--popover))",
          foreground: "hsl(var(--popover-foreground))",
        },
        card: {
          DEFAULT: "hsl(var(--card))",
          foreground: "hsl(var(--card-foreground))",
        },
        void: {
          DEFAULT: "hsl(var(--void))",
          deep: "hsl(var(--void-deep))",
        },
        sunrise: "hsl(var(--sunrise))",
        golden: {
          DEFAULT: "hsl(var(--golden))",
          soft: "hsl(var(--golden-soft))",
        },
        sidebar: {
          DEFAULT: "hsl(var(--sidebar-background))",
          foreground: "hsl(var(--sidebar-foreground))",
          primary: "hsl(var(--sidebar-primary))",
          "primary-foreground": "hsl(var(--sidebar-primary-foreground))",
          accent: "hsl(var(--sidebar-accent))",
          "accent-foreground": "hsl(var(--sidebar-accent-foreground))",
          border: "hsl(var(--sidebar-border))",
          ring: "hsl(var(--sidebar-ring))",
        },
      },
      borderRadius: {
        lg: "var(--radius)",
        md: "calc(var(--radius) - 2px)",
        sm: "calc(var(--radius) - 4px)",
      },
      keyframes: {
        "accordion-down": {
          from: { height: "0" },
          to: { height: "var(--radix-accordion-content-height)" },
        },
        "accordion-up": {
          from: { height: "var(--radix-accordion-content-height)" },
          to: { height: "0" },
        },
        "ticker-scroll": {
          "0%": { transform: "translateX(0)" },
          "100%": { transform: "translateX(-50%)" },
        },
      },
      animation: {
        "accordion-down": "accordion-down 0.2s ease-out",
        "accordion-up": "accordion-up 0.2s ease-out",
        "ticker-scroll": "ticker-scroll 45s linear infinite",
      },
    },
  },
  plugins: [require("tailwindcss-animate"), require("@tailwindcss/typography")],
} satisfies Config;

{
  "buildCommand": "npm run build",
  "outputDirectory": "dist",
  "framework": "vite",
  "headers": [
    {
      "source": "/(.*)",
      "headers": [
        { "key": "Content-Security-Policy", "value": "default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-eval' 'unsafe-inline'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' https://fonts.googleapis.com; img-src 'self' data: https:; font-src 'self' data: https://fonts.gstatic.com; media-src 'self' data: blob: https://*.supabase.co; connect-src 'self' https://*.supabase.co https://*.supabase.com wss://*.supabase.co wss://*.supabase.com https://api.groq.com https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com https://*.elevenlabs.io https://*.deepgram.com; worker-src 'self' blob:; manifest-src 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none';" },
        { "key": "X-Frame-Options", "value": "DENY" },
        { "key": "X-Content-Type-Options", "value": "nosniff" },
        { "key": "Referrer-Policy", "value": "strict-origin-when-cross-origin" },
        { "key": "Permissions-Policy", "value": "geolocation=(), microphone=(), camera=()" },
        { "key": "Strict-Transport-Security", "value": "max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains" }
      ]
    },
    {
      "source": "/assets/(.*)",
      "headers": [
        { "key": "Cache-Control", "value": "public, max-age=31536000, immutable" }
      ]
    },
    {
      "source": "/(.*)\\.(?:js|css|woff2?|png|jpe?g|webp|avif|gif|svg|ico)$",
      "headers": [
        { "key": "Cache-Control", "value": "public, max-age=31536000, immutable" }
      ]
    },
    {
      "source": "/version.json",
      "headers": [
        { "key": "Cache-Control", "value": "no-cache, no-store, must-revalidate" }
      ]
    },
    {
      "source": "/index.html",
      "headers": [
        { "key": "Cache-Control", "value": "no-cache, no-store, must-revalidate" }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "rewrites": [
    { "source": "/(.*)", "destination": "/index.html" }
  ]
}

===== eslint.config.js =====
import js from "@eslint/js";
import globals from "globals";
import reactHooks from "eslint-plugin-react-hooks";
import reactRefresh from "eslint-plugin-react-refresh";
import tseslint from "typescript-eslint";

export default tseslint.config(
  { ignores: ["dist"] },
  {
    extends: [js.configs.recommended, ...tseslint.configs.recommended],
    files: ["**/*.{ts,tsx}"],
    languageOptions: {
      ecmaVersion: 2020,
      globals: globals.browser,
    },
    plugins: {
      "react-hooks": reactHooks,
      "react-refresh": reactRefresh,
    },
    rules: {
      ...reactHooks.configs.recommended.rules,
      "react-refresh/only-export-components": ["warn", { allowConstantExport: true }],
      "@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars": "off",
    },
  },
);

===== vitest.config.ts =====
import { defineConfig } from "vitest/config";
import react from "@vitejs/plugin-react-swc";
import path from "path";

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [react()],
  test: {
    environment: "jsdom",
    globals: true,
    setupFiles: ["./src/test/setup.ts"],
    include: ["src/**/*.{test,spec}.{ts,tsx}"],
  },
  resolve: {
    alias: { "@": path.resolve(__dirname, "./src") },
  },
});

{
  "$schema": "https://ui.shadcn.com/schema.json",

    "components": "@/components",
    "utils": "@/lib/utils",
    "ui": "@/components/ui",
    "lib": "@/lib",
    "hooks": "@/hooks"
  }
